(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
§ 32.46 CAPITAL ASSET ACQUISITIONS.
   (A)   The method of acquisition is not a determining factor. Each department
should report items acquired by:
      (1)   Regular purchases;
      (2)   Lease purchase (see division (B) below);
      (3)   Construction by town personnel;
      (4)   Construction by an outside contractor;
      (5)   Resolution/condemnation;
      (6)   Donation/contribution;
      (7)   Addition to an existing asset;
      (8)   Transfer from another department;
      (9)   Trade or barter; and
      (10)   Annexation.
   (B)   (1)   Leased equipment should be capitalized if the lease agreement
meets any one of the following criteria:
         (a)   The lease transfers ownership of the property to the lessee by
the end of the lease term;
         (b)   The lease contains a bargain purchase option;
         (c)   The lease term is equal to 75% of the estimated economic life of
the leased property; or
         (d)   The present value of the minimum lease payments at the inception
of the lease, excluding executory costs, equals at least 90% of the fair value
of the leased property.
      (2)   Leases that do not meet any of the above criteria should be
recorded as an operating lease and reported in the notes of the financial
statements.
(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
§ 32.47 ASSET TRANSFERS AND DISPOSITIONS.
   (A)   Property should not be transferred, turned-in for auction, or disposed
of without prior approval of the department head. A vehicle/equipment out-
processing checklist should be sent to the Town Clerk-Treasurer's Office in all
cases. This form is a dual-purpose form for transfer (defined as any movement
of an asset by virtue of change in location, either by account, department,
building, floor, or room) or retirement (disposal) of property. The main points
to be remembered when using this form are:
      (1)   Always provide sufficient detail to properly identify the asset,
most importantly the asset's tag number or town ID;
      (2)   Be accurate and do not overlook any of the needed entries;
      (3)   Write legibly;
      (4)   Complete each column for every asset listed on the form;
      (5)   Enter information in correct row, depending on whether you are
transferring or deleting an asset;
      (6)   Have the department head sign at the bottom of the form; and
      (7)   Return the form to the Town Clerk-Treasurer's Office.
   (B)   If an asset is stolen, the department should notify the Safety Manager
as well as the Town Clerk-Treasurer's Office.
(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
§ 32.48 PERIODIC INVENTORIES.
   A physical inventory of all capital assets (any item over $5,000) will be
conducted in each department on or about December 31 of every year. The Town
Clerk-Treasurer's office will conduct spot checks on a random basis. Department
heads will be accountable for the capital asset inventory charged to their
departments by verifying a list of their capital assets at year-end.
(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
§ 32.49 RESPONSIBILITIES OF CLERK-TREASURER'S OFFICE.
   The Fiscal Officer will ensure that accounting for capital assets is being
exercised by establishing a capital asset inventory, both initially and
periodically in subsequent years. The Fiscal Officer will further ensure that
the capital asset report will be updated annually to reflect additions,
retirements, and transfers and to reflect the new, annual capital asset balance
for financial reporting purposes and the annual and accumulated depreciation
calculation.
(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
§ 32.50 RESPONSIBILITIES OF DEPARTMENT HEADS.
   (A)   It is the responsibility of the department head to act as or designate
a steward for each piece of property. The steward will become the focal point
for questions regarding the availability, condition, and usage of the asset, as
well as the contact during the physical inventory process.
   (B)   The steward shall be designated as the person to record the receipt of
the asset, to examine the asset to make sure that no damage was incurred during
shipment and to make sure that the asset was received in working order.
   (C)   The steward is also responsible for arranging for the necessary
preventative maintenance and any needed repairs to keep the asset in working
condition. It is necessary to have a responsible person available for questions
that arise during a physical inventory or when someone wants to borrow the
asset. The steward ensures that the asset is used for the purpose for which it
was acquired and that there is no personal or unauthorized use. In addition,
the steward should report any property damage or theft.
(Ord. 2020-12, passed 8-11-2020)
*** REMOTE PARTICIPATION ***
§ 32.70 REMOTE PARTICIPATION IN MEETINGS FOR REGULAR BUSINESS AND DURING
CERTAIN EMERGENCIES.
   (A)   In the ordinary course of business, the following policy shall govern
remote/electronic participation:
      (1)   Subject to division (1)(a), a member of the governing body who is
not physically present at a meeting of the governing body may participate in a
meeting by any electronic means of communication that does the following:
         (a)   Allows all participating members of the governing body to
simultaneously communicate with each other.
         (b)   Allows the public to simultaneously attend and observe the
meeting. However, this division does not apply to a meeting held in executive
session.
      (2)   A governing body member who participates in the meeting by an
electronic means of communication shall be considered present for purposes of
establishing a quorum but may participate in any final action taken at the
meeting only if the member can be seen and heard.
      (3)   A technological failure in an electronic means of communication
that disrupts or prevents:
         (a)   The simultaneous communication between a member who is not
physically present at the meeting and the governing body;
         (b)   A member of the public who is not present at the meeting from
attending and observing the meeting; or
         (c)   Does not prevent the governing body from conducting the meeting
or affect the validity of an action taken by the governing body at the meeting
if the sum of the governing body members physically present at the meeting and
the governing body members participating by electronic communication without
technological failure satisfy the quorum and (if a final action is taken) the
voting requirements of the governing body.
      (4)   All requirements under Indiana state law must be followed whether
or not referenced herein, but no more restrictive measures are adopted.
      (5)   For any meeting conducted with electronic participation the meeting
minutes shall include a statement of:
         (a)   The name of each member of the governing body who:
            1.   Was physically present at the place where the meeting was
conducted;
            2.   Participated in the meeting by using any electronic means of
communication; and
            3.   Was absent; and
         (b)   Identify the electronic means of communication by which:
            1.   Members of the governing body participated in the meeting; and
            2.   The public attended and observed the meeting, if the meeting
was not held in executive session.
      (6)   Members participating remotely shall be included in any roll call
and shall announce that they are participating remotely.
      (7)   All votes taken during a meeting under this section must be taken
by roll call vote.
      (8)   At least 50% of the members of the governing body must be
physically present at a meeting.
      (9)   A member of the governing body may not attend more than 50% of the
governing body's meetings in a calendar year by means of electronic
communication, unless the member's electronic participation is due to:
         (a)   Military service;
         (b)   Illness or other medical condition;
         (c)   Death of a relative; or
         (d)   An emergency involving actual or threatened injury to persons or
property.
      (10)   A member of a governing body may not participate in a meeting of
the governing body by electronic communication if the governing body is
attempting to take final action to:
         (a)   Adopt a budget;
         (b)   Make a reduction in personnel;
         (c)   Initiate a referendum;
         (d)   Establish or increase a fee;
         (e)   Establish or increase a penalty;
         (f)   Use the governing body's eminent domain authority; or
         (g)   Establish, raise, or renew a tax.
      (11)   A governing body may not prohibit a member of the governing body
from attending consecutive meetings by electronic communication. A member may
attend two consecutive meetings (a set of meetings) by electronic
communication. A member shall physically attend at least one meeting between
sets of meetings that the member attends by electronic communication, unless
the member's absence is due to:
         (a)   Military service;
         (b)   Illness or other medical condition;
         (c)   Death of a relative; or
         (d)   An emergency involving actual or threatened injury to persons or
property.
   (B)   In an emergency the following shall apply:
      (1)   This section applies only if:
         (a)   The governor declares a disaster emergency under I.C. 10-14-3-
12; or
         (b)   The executive (as defined in I.C. 36-1-2-5) of a political
subdivision declares a local disaster emergency under I.C. 10-14-3-29.
      (2)   Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, the members of a
governing body are not required to be physically present at a meeting until the
disaster emergency or local disaster emergency is terminated. The members of a
governing body may meet by any means of electronic communication, if the
following are satisfied:
         (a)   At least a quorum of the members of the governing body
participate in the meeting by means of electronic communication or in person.
         (b)   The public is able to simultaneously attend and observe the
meeting. However, this division does not apply to a meeting held in executive
session.
         (c)   The minutes of an emergency meeting so held must include:
            1.   The name of each member of the governing body who:
               a.   Participated in the meeting by using any electronic means
of communication; and
               b.   Was absent; and
            2.   Identify the electronic means of communication by which:
               a.   Members of the governing body participated in the meeting;
and
               b.   The public attended and observed the meeting, if the
meeting was not held in executive session.
      (3)   Members participating remotely shall be included in any roll call
and shall announce that they are participating remotely.
      (4)   All votes taken during a meeting under this section must be taken
by roll call vote.
(Res. 2022-3, passed 7-27-2022)
*** RULES FOR PUBLIC PARTICIPATION ***
§ 32.90 QUORUM AND AUTHORIZATION.
   A quorum consists of a majority of the entire membership of the Town Council
who are qualified to vote. Action of the Town Council is not official, unless
it is authorized, by a majority of the entire membership of the Town Council at
a regular or special meeting. In the event of two-to-two tie vote, the Clerk-
Treasurer may vote to break such tie.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.91 MEETINGS.
   All meetings shall be open to the public, except as otherwise permitted by
law and will procedurally follow Roberts Rules of Order, as best as possible.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.92 REGULAR MEETINGS.
   Regular meetings shall be held on the first and third Thursday of each
month, unless otherwise scheduled. A calendar of the regular meetings shall be
prepared at the beginning of each year and may be amended from time to time.
Meetings will be called to order at the discretion of the President at 7:00
p.m. at a location to be noticed. Regular meetings may be cancelled due to a
lack of any agenda items.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.93 SPECIAL MEETING.
   Special meetings shall be held upon call of the President, Vice-President,
Clerk-Treasurer or as determined at a regular meeting. The Clerk-Treasurer
shall send to all members, at least two days in advance of a special meeting, a
written notice fixing the time and place of the meeting.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.94 OFFICERS OF THE TOWN COUNCIL.
   (A)   The President and Vice-President shall be elected at the first regular
meeting held each year and will serve in their roles for a six month term to
expire on June 30 of the same year. Prior to June 30, the Council shall elect
the President and Vice-President for the remaining half of the calendar year
(July 1 through December 31).
   (B)   The President, subject to these rules, shall decide all points of
procedure unless otherwise directed by a majority of the members of the Town
Council.
   (C)   The Vice-President shall serve in the absence of the President.
   (D)   A temporary President shall be appointed in case both President and
Vice-President are absent.
   (E)   The Clerk-Treasurer shall be the Clerk-Treasurer, pursuant to Indiana
Code.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023; Res. 2023-3, passed 6-15-2023)
§ 32.95 DUTIES OF SECRETARY.
   The Clerk-Treasurer shall have the duties proscribed under Indiana law.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.96 AGENDA.
   The Town Council may follow an agenda. If such an agenda is used, it must be
posted. The Town Council may deviate from such agenda at its discretion, by
vote or by the course of its conduct. The President, Vice-President or majority
vote of the Town Council may add or remove items to the agenda during the
meeting. Prior to the meeting, agenda items may be added by the agreement of
President, Vice-President, or two members. Any item not on the agenda or added
properly according to these rules is not eligible for a vote of the Board.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.97 PUBLIC INPUT AT PUBLIC HEARINGS AND PUBLIC MEETINGS.
   (A)   The Town Council shall hold public hearings as required by law. This
section addresses procedures for public input at both public hearings and
public meetings.
   (B)   Persons with questions or comments may raise them, addressed to the
Town Council, during the public hearing. The citizen's remarks/observations are
to be constructive in nature or to bring attention to specific matters. The
Town Council is not obligated to answer any question raised, but may do so to
make clarifications or otherwise encourage well-informed and orderly
consideration of the matter at hand. Citizens may not use this venue to make a
speech to the public.
   (C)   As a general rule, public comments shall be allotted up to two minutes
per person and citizens may speak up to twice on any motion. These limits are
meant to encourage public participation in local government, but also avoid
endless debate.
   (D)   A speaker may request additional time for good cause shown, and the
Town Council may grant, deny or otherwise address such request. The President
may cut short any presentations that are irrelevant, repetitive, slanderous,
offensive, or that are otherwise not conducive to an orderly proceeding,
provided that the presenter has been admonished at least once.
   (E)   Every person appearing at hearings shall abide by the order and
directives of the President. Discourteous, disorderly, or contemptuous conduct
shall be regarded as a breach of the privileges of the Town Council.
   (F)   Citizen's remarks or observations are limited to remarks/observations
that relate to the scope of authority granted the Town Council under Indiana
Law or that directly relate to the purpose of the public hearing.
   (G)   The board will be able to research and to respond better if complex or
complicated remarks or questions are submitted in advance.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.98 ORDER OF BUSINESS AT REGULAR MEETINGS.
   (A)   The order of business at all regular meetings of the Town Council
shall be as follows:
      (1)   Roll call.
      (2)   Approval of minutes of previous meeting.
      (3)   Unfinished business.
      (4)   New business.
   (B)   The Council President may alter the order of business as necessary to
conduct an orderly proceeding.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.99 PRIOR RULES.
   Any rules which may have been previously passed, setting forth the
procedures and duties of the Town Council, are hereby repealed.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.100 CONFLICT WITH STATE LAW.
   In the event that a conflict should arise between these Rules of Procedure
and Indiana Code, Indiana Code shall govern.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.101 SUSPENSION OF THE RULES.
   These Rules of Procedure are set forth to assist the Town Council in
conducting its business and to provide reasonable consistency. The Town Council
may deviate at any time from these rules by motion or by conduct. If a member
of the Town Council objects at the time of any deviation, a vote shall be
called to approve or reject such deviation, otherwise the deviation shall be
deemed accepted and any challenge waived. No deviation from these Rules of
Procedure shall give rise to any complaint or any cause of action. (However,
nothing in these rules shall remove or lessen a right or procedure required
under Indiana law.)
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)
§ 32.102 AMENDMENTS.
   Amendments to these Rules of Procedure may be made by the Town Council at
any regular or special meeting upon the affirmative vote of a majority of the
members of the Town Council.
(Res. 2023-1, passed 2-16-2023)

*** GENERAL PROVISIONS ***
§ 50.01 REMOVAL FROM THE STATE UTILITY REGULATORY COMMISSION.
   The municipally owned water utilities of the town shall be hereby removed
from the jurisdiction of the State Utility Regulatory Commission for approval
of rates and charges and of the issuance of stock, bonds, notes or other
evidence of indebtedness.
(Ord. 1-1993, passed 7-27-1993)
*** WATER UTILITY ***

§ 50.16 RULES AND REGULATIONS GENERALLY.
   The following rules and regulations governing the operation of the municipal
water utility are hereby adopted as follows.
(1980 Code, § 14-2)
§ 50.17 METER DEPOSIT.
   (A)   Each consumer of the Water Utility and the Sewage Works, before being
connected to the system, shall be required to make a deposit with the town in
the sum of $200. Said deposit shall be returned to the customer after one year
of satisfactory payment of charges, or when the customer has terminated
service, provided all charges due and owing from the customer are paid.
   (B)   Otherwise, the deposit shall be used to offset any charge due and
owing by the customer.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 5-2008, passed 1-13-2009; Ord. 2022-4, passed 3-1-
2022)

   (A)   Each user of the Water Utility, upon being connected to the system,
shall pay a connection or tapping charge for such connection, which charge
shall be made directly to the user according to the following schedule, which
may be amended by the Town Council from time to time.

   The Utility Department Head shall approve all connections and taps before
any construction begins. In addition, consumer shall be charged a $25
inspection fee.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001; Ord. 2022-26, passed 2-21-
2023)
§ 50.20 EXTENSIONS.
   Extensions of all distribution or transmission lines shall be made in
accordance with the Rules and Standards of Service for Water Utilities of
Indiana, as now in effect or as hereinafter amended.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.21 SEPARATE USER METERING.
   Each user shall be separately metered, whether such user shall be located
within one structure or a number of structures, and even though located on
multiple lots or within the boundaries of a single lot; except that, any
customer now receiving water for or on behalf of more than one user through a
single meter shall, at his or her option, continue on a single meter until
ownership changes or additional users are added. All changes and additions are
to be approved by the Water Superintendent prior to construction. For purposes
of this section, a USER shall mean any single commercial establishment or any
single residence family.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.22 COLLECTION OF DEFERRED PAYMENT CHARGE.
   All bids for water service not paid within 15 days from the due date
thereof, as stated in such bills, shall be subject to a collection of deferred
payment charge of 10% on the first $3 and 3% on amounts in excess of $3.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.23 RECONNECTION CHARGE.
   Each user shall be charged $25 for the reconnection of service because of
non-payment. This cost is based on, but not limited to, the approximate cost of
labor and transportation incurred to accomplish reconnection. Whenever the
Water Utility has turned off the water service, the customer shall not turn it
on, nor cause it to be turned on without permission of the Water Superintendent
or his or her representative.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.24 METERS.
   The Water Utility will specify and furnish all meters, except by agreement
with the Water Superintendent. Any damage caused by the customer or owner, or
by others with their consent, because of illegal access, tampering or any other
actions causing damage to the meter or setting, whether intentional or not, the
cost of repairs will be charged to the customer and/or owner. Non-payment of
these charges shall be reason for discontinuance of service until such fees are
paid.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2007, passed 8-28-2007)
§ 50.25 ACCESS TO METERS AND EQUIPMENT.
   The Water Utility shall have access, at all reasonable hours, to replace,
inspect or otherwise maintain all meters, valves, hydrants and other equipment
associated with water service. It is also forbidden for the users to cover up
or in any way obstruct the free access to any water meter, hydrant, valve or
other equipment required for town water service. In case of a violation of this
provision, and failure on the part of the owner of the premises to remove such
obstruction upon notice from the Water Utility, the water service shall be
discontinued.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.26 MAINTENANCE OF SERVICE.
   The Water Utility assumes responsibility for maintaining that part of the
service that connects, and includes, the corporation stop, service line up to
and including the meter setting, meter, pit and lid, including any meters
located on the premises. The remainder of the service from the meter, or curb
stops, to the user’s plumbing is to be maintained and kept in repair by the
customer. Any changes in location of the meter setting or curb box shall be at
the customer’s expense.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.27 PROPERTY OWNERS RESPONSIBLE FOR BILLS.
   The rates and charges may be billed to the tenant or tenants occupying the
properties served by the Water Utility, unless otherwise requested in writing
by the owner, but such billing shall in no way relieve the owner from the
liability in the event payment is not made as herein required.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.28 BILLING.
   (A)   Bills for water service furnished through meters shall be rendered
monthly. A MONTH shall not mean a calendar month, but shall mean the period
between any two attempted consecutive readings of the meter by the Utility, and
shall be as nearly 30 days as practicable.
   (B)   The Water Utility will make an effort to read meters once a month and
such readings shall be prima facie evidence of the amount of water used. If the
Utility is unable to gain access to a customer’s property from obstructions,
inclement weather or other difficulties, the Utility shall estimate consumption
for the period. Bills so made shall be labeled “estimated” and the difference,
if any, shall be adjusted with the next regular reading. If the meter fails to
register, in whole or part, the customer shall pay at the average rate as shown
by the three previous readings.
   (C)   The Water Utility will undertake to deliver bills and notices by
depositing it in the Post Office only as a matter of convenience to the owner
or consumer; failure to receive bills shall not relieve any person from his or
her obligation in the payment of such bills.
   (D)   There shall be no abatement in charges by reason of non-use of service
by customer unless the Utility has been ordered to discontinue service.
   (E)   An adjustment to the sewage charges, or “sprinkling rate”, will be
made during the months of July, August and September for residential customers.
Sewage charges will be based on water usage during January, February and March.
Any other adjustments to sewage charges due to outside leaks, filling swimming
pools, construction use, commercial/industrial use and the like must be
approved by the Water Utility Superintendent.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.29 DISCONTINUANCE OF SERVICE.
   A customer’s water service may be discontinued by the Water Utility at the
tap or curb for any of the following reasons:
   (A)   For any use of water not covered by contract;
   (B)   For any tampering or knowingly permitting any tampering with any
service pipe, curb stop, meter, meter seal, fire hydrant or sprinkler system;
   (C)   It is the policy of the town to discontinue utility service to
customers by reason of nonpayment of bills only after notice and a meaningful
opportunity to be heard on disputed bills. The town’s form for application for
utility service and all bills shall contain, in addition to the title, address,
room number, and telephone number of the official in charge of billing, clearly
visible and easily readable provisions to the effect:
      (1)   That all bills are due and payable on or before the date set forth
on the bill.
      (2)   That if any bill is not paid by or before that date, a second bill
will be mailed containing a cutoff notice that if the bill is not paid within
ten days of the mailing of the second bill, service will be discontinued for
nonpayment.
      (3)   That any customer disputing the correctness of his or her bill
shall have a right to a hearing at which time he or she may be represented in
person and by counsel or any other person of his or her choosing and may
present orally or in writing his or her complaint and contentions to the town
official in charge of utility billing. This official shall be authorized to
order that the customer’s service not be discontinued and shall have the
authority to make a final determination of the customer’s complaint.
      (4)   Requests for delays or waiver of payment will not be entertained;
only questions of proper and correct billing will be considered. In the absence
of payment of the bill rendered or resort to the hearing procedure provided
herein, service will be discontinued at the time specified, but in no event
until the charges have been due and unpaid for at least 30 days.
      (5)   When it becomes necessary for the town to discontinue utility
service to a customer for nonpayment of bills, service will be reinstated only
after all bills for service then due have been paid, along with a turn-on
charge in the sum of $25.
   (D)   For failures to provide free and non-hazardous access to the meter so
that representative of Utility may take meter readings, make necessary
inspections, maintain, replace or remove meter;
   (E)   For unapproved cross-connections with any other source of water
supply;
   (F)   For permitting any condition to exist about the premises, which might,
in the opinion of the Water Utility, cause pollution of the public water
supply;
   (G)   For wastage of un-metered water; and/or
   (H)   For violation of any of these rules or regulations or any amendments
thereof.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.30 FIRE LINES AND HYDRANTS.
   (A)   All pipes or hydrants placed in or about buildings for fire protection
shall be used exclusively for fire purposes.
   (B)   No person, not an authorized employee of the Water Utility, Fire
Department or Street Department, shall open, operate or remove the nozzle cap
from any public or private fire hydrant to which either is supplied by the
Utility. Temporary connections to fire hydrants for industrial or other
purposes shall be permitted only when authorized by the Water Superintendent or
his or her representative.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.31 SHUT-OFF VALVE FOR EMERGENCY USE REQUIRED.
   The owner of every building where the Water Utility provides water service
shall provide a good shut-off valve located in a convenient location just
inside the basement or crawl space so that the occupants in case of emergency
may shut off the water.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.32 FLUORIDATION.
   The Town Council is authorized to provide the means for and to proceed with
the addition of sufficient quantities of a fluoride compound to the public
water supply of the town Water Utility to bring the total amount of fluoride
ions present in the furnished water to the optimum concentration recommended by
the State Board of Health. The Water Utility shall keep an accurate record of
the amount of fluoride-bearing chemical applied and the quantity of water
treated, and shall conduct such analytical tests to be made for fluoride in the
treated water as shall be directed by the State Board of Health.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
§ 50.33 WATER LEAK ADJUSTMENT POLICY.
   (A)   Eligibility. For the customers to be eligible for a leak adjustment
the following factors must be met:
      (1)   A customer's leak is determined as a loss of water that exceeds
100% of the customer's average water usage for the proceeding six months;
      (2)   That the customer upon discovering the leak or becoming aware of
the leak notified the water utility within seven business days and immediately
had the leak repaired;
      (3)   That the leak occurred between the water meter or the curb stop and
point of entry into the building or dwelling; and
      (4)   That the customer has not had a previous water leak adjustment
during the current calendar year.
   (B)   Ineligibility. For the customers to be ineligible for a leak
adjustment the following factors must be met:
      (1)   Leaks not meeting the above requirements or unexplained leaks/
usage;
      (2)   Leaks due to failure of internal plumbing, including but not
limited to leaking faucets, leaking toilets, leaking appliances, and the like;
      (3)   Homes that utilize irrigation systems shall not be eligible for
leak adjustments, unless it can be established that the leak did not occur on/
in the irrigation system; and
      (4)   If at any time the town notifies the owner/account holder of a
possible leak, or if the owner/account holder knows of a leak, and the owner/
account holder fails to act within seven days, the town may determine the leak
adjustment is ineligible.
   (C)   Responsibility of the consumer.
      (1)   The consumer is responsible for contacting Brookville Water Works
regarding the leak and shall provide necessary documentation of leak repairs
from a qualified contractor.
      (2)   The consumer is responsible for contacting Brookville Water Works
to request the proposed adjustment and certify via an affidavit that there was
a valid leak.
      (3)   Leak adjustment requests must be made in writing within 30 days
after the consumer receives his/her monthly bill.
      (4)   No adjustments will be automatically applied.
   (D)   Procedure to request a leak adjustment.
      (1)   Upon being presented with the leak affidavit, the Clerk-Treasurer
or Town Administrator will determine the validity of the leak adjustment
request.
      (2)   If the Town Administrator or Water Superintendent refuses the
adjustment and the consumer is not satisfied, then he/she may request that the
matter be taken before the Town Council. The Council will conduct a hearing at
a regularly scheduled Council meeting and shall in its discretion make a final
determination.
   (E)   Calculation of leak adjustment.
      (1)   The consumer shall be charged the prevailing rate for the six month
average usage;
      (2)   The sales tax will be adjusted to the applicable amount based on
the adjusted water bill;
      (3)   The sewer bill will be adjusted based on the gallons used for six
month average usage;
      (4)   If the consumer requesting the leak adjustment does not have six
months of water history, the adjustment will be calculated based upon the
readings from the previous water account.
   (F)   Upon receiving an adjustment, the customer shall pay the bill within
the regular billing cycle. If the customer fails to make payment it is
considered delinquent and will be handled in the same manner as the regular
monthly billing delinquencies.
   (G)   (1)   If the leak adjustment is denied by the utility or by Town
Council, the consumer must pay the unadjusted bill within the regular billing
cycle.
      (2)   Penalties incurred as a result of delinquent payment will not be
adjusted and/or removed from the bill per the Indiana State Board of Accounts,
unless approved by Town Council
(Ord. 2020-14, passed 10-13-2020)
§ 50.34 CROSS CONNECTIONS PROHIBITED; BACKFLOW PREVENTION.
   (A)   A CROSS CONNECTION shall be defined as any physical connection or
arrangement between two otherwise separate systems, one of which contains
potable water from the town water system, and the other, water from a private
source, water of unknown or questionable safety, or steam, gases, or chemicals,
whereby there may be flow from one system to the other, the direction of flow
depending on the pressure differential between two systems.
   (B)   No person, firm, or corporation shall establish or permit to be
established or maintain or permit to be maintained any cross connection. No
cross connection shall be established whereby potable water from a private,
auxiliary, or emergency water supply other than the regular public water supply
of the town may enter the supply or distribution system of said town, unless
such private, auxiliary, or emergency water supply and the method of connection
and use of such supply shall have been approved by the Water/Wastewater
Utilities and by the Indiana Department of Environmental Management in
accordance with 327 IAC 8-10.
   (C)   It shall be the duty of the Water/Wastewater Utilities to cause
inspections to be made of all properties served by the public water system
where cross connections with the public water system is deemed possible. The
frequency of inspections and re-inspections based on potential health hazards
involved shall be established by the Water/Wastewater Utilities.
   (D)   Upon presentation of credentials, the representative of the Water/
Wastewater Utilities shall have the right to request entry at any reasonable
time to examine the property served by a connection to the public water system
of the town for cross connections. On request, the owner, lessee, or occupant
of any property so served shall furnish to the representative of the Water/
Wastewater Utilities any pertinent information regarding the piping system or
systems on such property. The refusal of access or refusal of requested
pertinent information shall be deemed evidence of the presence of cross
connections on the property.
   (E)   The Water/Wastewater Utilities are hereby authorized and directed to
discontinue water service to any property wherein any connection in violation
of this section exists, and to take such other precautionary measures deemed
necessary to eliminate any danger of contamination of the public water system.
Water service shall be discontinued only after reasonable notice is served on
the owner, lessee, or occupants of the property or premises where a violation
is found or suspected to exist. Water service to such property shall not be
restored until the cross connection(s) has been eliminated in compliance with
the provisions of this section. Violations of this section are also subject to
the penalties outlined in §
50.99 of this chapter.
   (F)   If it is deemed by the Water/Wastewater Utilities that a cross
connection or an emergency endangers public health, safety, and/or welfare and
requires immediate action, and a written finding to that effect is filed with
the Clerk-Treasure of the town and delivered to the owner, lessee, or occupant
at the property/premises, service may be immediately discontinued. The owner,
lessee, or occupant shall have an opportunity for a hearing before the Town
Council by providing a written request for said hearing to the Clerk-Treasurer
within ten days of the discontinuation of service. The hearing will take place
at the next regularly scheduled meeting of the Town Council after written
notice has been given to the Clerk-Treasurer.
   (G)   All owners, lessees, or occupants of properties served by the town's
public water system that use toxic or hazardous liquids, all hospitals,
mortuaries, wastewater treatment plants, laboratories, and all other hazardous
owners, lessees, or occupants of properties served by the town's public water
system shall install and maintain a reduce pressure principal backflow
preventer in the main water line serving each structure on the property/
premises. The backflow preventer must be installed in an easily accessible
location that is not subject to flooding or freezing. The reduced pressure
principle backflow preventers shall not be installed below ground level.
   (H)   This section does not supersede the Indiana Plumbing Code, the IDEM
Rule 327 IAC 8- 10, or any other chapter and/or section of this code, but is
supplementary to them.
   (I)   A backflow prevention device shall be required at all customer
facilities outlined in 327 IAC 8-10-4(c), as the same may be amended from time
to time, unless an exemption has been issued for the customer facility pursuant
to 327 IAC 8-10-4(e).
   (J)   If, in the judgment of the town's Utilities Superintendent, an
approved backflow prevention device is necessary for the safety of the public
water system, the Utilities Superintendent will give notice to the owner,
lessee, or occupant of the property utilizing the public water system to
install an approved backflow prevention device immediately. The owner, lessee,
or occupant shall, at their own expense, install such an approved backflow
prevention device at a location and in a manner approved by the Utilities
Superintendent and shall have inspections and tests made of such approved
devices as required by the Utilities Superintendent and in accordance with IDEM
Rule 327 IAC 8-10.
(Ord. 2025-14, passed 6-16-2025)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   For violations of §
50.23 of this chapter, a penalty of $25 for first violation, $50 for second
violation and no reinstatement of service for third violation will be imposed
on violators.
(1980 Code, § 14-2) (Ord. 3-2001, passed 8-28-2001)
