Pretreatment Program must be inspected at least three times per year. However,
many facilities will receive numerous inspections/visits during a given year to
track compliance schedule activities, verify changes in discharge or processes,
maintain a regulatory presence, or follow up accidental discharges.
   (B)   Scheduling shall be done on a random basis and tracked and logged on
an inspection tracking file. Depending upon the situation, advance notice by
telephone may be given but shall not be required.
   (C)   Other inspections shall be conducted according to needs or for
verification of compliance.
   (D)   All insignificant users shall be inspected yearly unless they are
removed from further survey. To be removed from further inspections, a facility
must be inspected and deemed as non-impact and strictly domestic discharger.
This data shall be updated annually and provided to the control authority
(IDEM).
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
**** DIVISION 12. ENFORCEMENT RESPONSE PROCEDURES ****
§ 51.315 ENFORCEMENT RESPONSE PROCEDURES DEFINED.
   (A)   The enforcement response procedures (ERP) contains detailed procedures
on how the town’s pretreatment program investigates and responds to instances
of industrial user (IU) and/or non-residential noncompliance. It is to be
understood that if an industrial user’s noncompliance persists after
notification by the pretreatment group, the Indiana Department of Environmental
Management may proceed with enforcement action directly against the user. IDEM
may also take its own enforcement action independently of the town or in
conjunction with other enforcement actions.
   (B)   With this in mind, action taken by the town is preferable to that of
IDEM’s. EPA retains its authority to take its own enforcement action where the
state or town has not taken timely or appropriate action.
   (C)   These procedures have been promulgated by the EPA, to include ranges
of enforcement responses available to the town. In any specific case, factors
may warrant different responses than those contained in these procedures for a
variety of reasons, such as, the degree of variance from pretreatment
standards, duration of violation, previous enforcement actions, and the
deterrent effect of the response.
   (D)   These procedures address a broad range of pretreatment violations. It
is not the intention of these procedures, therein, to limit the enforcement
discretion of any of the administering agencies.
   (E)   It should be noted that the Clean Water Act clearly expresses that all
extraordinary efforts are required by industry to comply with pretreatment
standards.
   (F)   Enforcement of this subchapter shall be subject to enforcement actions
within the federal law, state law or town code, the severity of the violation
and the site operator’s efforts to comply. The town shall reserve the right to
interpret enforcement on a case by case basis. Tiered enforcement will be
practiced at the town’s discretion. The tiered enforcement may include:
      (1)   Verbal warning to the site operator to make corrections;
      (2)   Written warning to the site operator to make corrections within a
specified period of time. The period of time shall take into account issues
such as the severity of the problem, pending weather, seasonal conditions and
the level of effort necessary to correct the problem;
      (3)   Warning of non-compliance with directions to the site operator that
site conditions require immediate action;
      (4)   Stop work order; and
      (5)   Fines as may be permissible under the municipal code and this
subchapter .
   (G)   If abatement of a violation and/or restoration of affected property is
required, the notice shall set forth a deadline within which such remediation
or restoration must be completed. Said notice shall further advise that, should
the violator fail to remediate or restore within the established deadline, the
work will be done by a designated governmental agency or a contractor and the
expense shall be charged to the violator.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.316 ENFORCEMENT AUTHORITY.
   This subchapter shall be enforced by the town’s personnel, also known as the
control authority. The personnel shall be considered the Superintendent’s duly
authorized representatives in all enforcement activities, fines, and actions
relating to this subchapter.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.317 JUDGMENT CRITERIA.
   (A)   Industries and all non-residential users found not to be in compliance
with federal, state and local requirements are subject to the conditions of
these ordinance procedures. Informal enforcement actions, verbal telephone
notices, letters of violation, site visits, administrative orders, enforcement
compliance schedules and administrative fines shall be conducted by the
pretreatment group under the supervision of the Superintendent. Enforcement
actions which require legal action are made by the Town Council upon
recommendation of the Superintendent of the POTW.
   (B)   Violations and discrepancies that have been identified during the
review process shall be evaluated as to the type of enforcement response
necessary by the Superintendent of the POTW. In order to maintain equitable
treatment of violators and provide a stronger basis for selection of
appropriate responses to violations, the following enforcement response
procedures (ERP) will normally be followed, unless mitigating circumstances can
be shown.
   (C)   The enforcement response procedures identify the type of noncompliance
and circumstances which might vary the type of response for that particular
category of noncompliance. The enforcement response procedures have been
developed with the intention of serving three main purposes:
      (1)   It recommends enforcement responses that are appropriate in
relation to the nature and severity of the violation and overall degree of
noncompliance;
      (2)   It provides the procedures to encourage a uniform application of
enforcement responses to comparable levels in types of violations, and can be
used as a mechanism to review the appropriate response; and
      (3)   It allows the industrial community to realize the importance of the
pretreatment program along with proper operation of equipment, meeting of
standards and limits, along with responses by the pretreatment group, if, and
when various violations occur.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.318 TYPES OF ENFORCEMENT RESPONSE.
   (A)   In order to provide a concise manual in a usable format, acronyms are
used for several types of responses. The definitions for these acronyms are
listed below in increasing order of severity. They are:
      (1)   VTN - verbal telephone notice. Is meant to describe a response to a
very minor type of violation which is normally conveyed verbally to the contact
person at the industry and no further follow up normally is expected. An
example might be when there is a very minor infraction, such as a report being
received one or two days late with no other infractions.
      (2)   SV - site visit. A visit to the industrial site to discuss and
observe the problem. This may be a substitution for VTN or LOV. The SV may also
be made in conjunction with a letter of violation. The SV may also require a
response within ten days, indicating a reason for the noncompliance and what
steps are being taken to eliminate any future violations of this nature. A
field visit report shall be filled out upon each visit.   
      (3)   LOV - letter of violation. Is a written notification to the
industrial user indicating the type of apparent violation and requiring a
response within ten days, indicating a reason for the noncompliance and what
steps are being taken to eliminate any future violations of this nature.   
      (4)   AO - administrative order. An administrative order would be in such
cases where the noncompliance has been recognized and the Superintendent shall
require the industrial user to perform corrective actions.   
      (5)   AF - administrative fine.  An administrative fine would be in such
cases where all lower types of enforcement responses have failed and/or where
deemed appropriate by the Superintendent because of the nature and/or intent of
the violation. The next response step is court action. The administrative fine
step exists to try to prevent court activity and yet correct the problem, and/
or show the seriousness of the problem to the industry involved. The maximum
administrative fine shall be $1,000 per violation with each day being deemed a
separate violation. The administrative fine may also be part of an
administrative order, enforcement compliance schedule, or may be instituted as
the next step above an administrative order.   
      (6)   LIT - litigation.  Litigation is utilized to define several courses
of action, including civil suits for injunctive relief and/or civil penalties,
criminal suits, termination of service, and the like, these types of actions
would all involve the courts and would follow the procedures necessary for due
process.   
   (B)   Utilizing the enforcement response procedures, the Superintendent
shall initiate the appropriate response and see that the files have been
updated to show the type of action being taken. The Superintendent shall
initiate any field surveying which he or she feels appropriate to substantiate
previous data received, or to check the response of an industrial user to the
action which they have indicated that they have taken.
   (C)   At the end of any EPA established six-month period, the Superintendent
shall be responsible for reviewing the industry files to determine any SNCs,
and if so, the Superintendent shall be responsible for seeing that the SNCs are
tabulated on an annual basis and that the name of all SNCs are published in the
local daily newspaper.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.319 SIGNIFICANT NONCOMPLIANCE.
   Significant noncompliance (SNC) are instances which meet one or more of the
following criteria:
   (A)   Violations of wastewater discharge limits;
      (1)   Chronic violations: Sixty-six percent or more of the measurements
exceed the same daily maximum limit or the same average limit in a six month
period.
      (2)   Technical review criteria (TRC): Thirty-three percent or more of
the measurements exceed the same daily maximum limit or the same average limit
by more than the TRC in a six-month period. There are two groups of TRCs:
         (a)   Group I.  for conventional pollutants (BOD, TSS, fats, oil and
grease - animal or vegetable, non-regulated petroleum hydrocarbons, phosphorus,
ammonia, pH), TRC = 1.4 of daily maximum limits as defined in this subchapter.
         (b)   Group II. For all other pollutants, TRC = 1.2 of daily maximum
limits as defined in this subchapter.
      (3)   Any other violation or violations of an effluent limit (average or
daily maximum) that the Superintendent believes has caused, alone or in
combination with other discharges, interferences (such as, slug loads) or pass
through, or endangered the health of the sewage treatment personnel or the
public.
      (4)   Any discharge of a pollutant which has caused imminent endangerment
to human health/welfare or to the environment and resulted in the POTW’s
exercise of its emergency authority to halt or prevent such a discharge.
   (B)   Violations of compliance schedule milestones, contained in a local
control mechanism or enforcement order, for starting construction, completing
construction, and attaining final compliance by 90 days or more after the
schedule date.
   (C)   Failure to provide reports for any compliance schedules, self-
monitoring data, baseline monitoring reports and any other report due within 30
days from the due date.
   (D)   Failure to accurately report noncompliance.
   (E)   Any other violation or group of violations which the Superintendent
considers to be significant.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.320 TIME FRAMES FOR RESPONSES.
   (A)   All violations shall be identified and documented by the pretreatment
group within five working days of receiving noncompliance information.
   (B)   Any violation shall involve contact by the pretreatment group with the
industrial user and request of information on corrective or preventative
actions(s), and shall occur within 30 days of violation detection.
   (C)   When a compliance schedule is required, the pretreatment group shall
notify the IU of the requirement within 60 days.
   (D)   Violations which threaten health, property or environmental quality
shall be considered emergencies and shall receive immediate responses such as
halting the discharges or terminating service, as per discretion of the
Superintendent.
   (E)   Violations meeting the criteria for significant noncompliance (SNC)
shall be addressed with an enforcement action within 30 days of the
identification of significant noncompliance.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)

**** DIVISION 13. FEES ****

   Flat fees for pretreatment industrial monitoring shall be as follows:
Sample, each 24-hour composite sample      $75

(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.336 SURCHARGES ON STRONG WASTES.
   (A)   In order that the rates and charges may be justly and equitably
adjusted to the service rendered to users, the town shall base its charges not
only on the volume, but also on strength and character of the stronger-than-
normal domestic sewage and wastes which it is required to treat and dispose of.
The town shall require the user to determine the strength and content of all
sewage and wastes discharged, either directly or indirectly into the sanitary
sewage system, in such manner and by such method as the town may deem
practicable in light of the conditions and attending circumstances of the case,
in order to determine the proper charge. The user shall furnish a central
sampling point available to the town at all times.   
   (B)   Normal sewage domestic waste strength should not exceed a biochemical
oxygen demand of 200 milligrams per liter of fluid, suspended solids in excess
of 225 milligrams per liter of fluid, phosphorous in excess of ten milligrams
per liter of fluid, ammonia in excess of 20 milligrams per liter of fluid. When
the above mentioned pollutants are exceeded and where the surcharge amount
would be less than $10, a minimum monthly surcharge of $10 will be assessed.
Additional charges for treating stronger-than-normal domestic waste shall be
made on the following basis:
      (1)   Rate surcharge based upon suspended solids. There shall be an
additional charge of $0.15 per pound of suspended solids for suspended solids
received in excess of 225 milligrams per liter of fluid.
      (2)   Rate surcharge based upon BOD or COD.
         (a)   BOD. There shall be an additional charge of $0.05 per pound of
biochemical oxygen demand for BOD received in excess of 200 milligram per liter
of fluid.
         (b)   COD. There shall be an additional charge of $0.05 per pound of
chemical oxygen demand for COD received in excess of 500 milligrams per liter
of fluid.   
      (3)   Rate surcharge based upon phosphorous. There shall be an additional
charge of $3 per pound of phosphorous received in excess of ten milligrams per
liter of fluid.   
      (4)   Rate surcharge based upon ammonia. There shall be an additional
charge of $0.75 per pound of ammonia received in excess of 20 milligrams per
liter of fluid.
   (C)   The determination of suspended solids and five-day biochemical oxygen
demand, ammonia, COD and phosphorous contained in the waste shall be in
accordance with the latest copy of Standard Methods for the Examination of
Water, Sewage and Industrial Wastes, as written by the American Public Health
Association, the American Water Works Association and the Water Pollution
Control Federation, and in conformance with Guidelines Establishing Test
Procedures for Analysis of Pollutants, Regulation C.F.R. Part 136, published in
the Federal Register on October 16, 1973.
   (D)   As provided by statute, all rates and charges not paid when due are
hereby declared to be delinquent and penalty of 10% of the amount of the rates
or charges shall thereupon attach thereto. Such rates or charges shall be paid
within 15 days of mailing of the bill.   
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.337 REVIEW OF RATES.
   (A)   In order that the rates and charges for sewage services may remain
fair and equitable and be in proportion to the cost of providing services to
the various users or user classes, the town shall cause a study to be made
within a reasonable period of time following the first two years of operation
after the date on which this subchapter goes into effect. Such study shall
include, but not be limited to, an analysis of the costs associated with the
treatment of excessive strength effluents from industrial users, volume and
delivery flow rate characteristics attributed to the various users or user
classes, the financial position of the sewage works and the adequacy of its
revenue to provide reasonable funds for the operation and maintenance,
replacements, debt service requirements and capital improvements to the waste
treatment systems.   
   (B)   Thereafter, on a biennial basis, within a reasonable period of time
following the normal accounting period, the town shall cause a similar study to
be made for the purpose of reviewing the fairness and equity of the rates and
charges for sewage services on a continuing basis. The studies shall be
conducted by officers of employees of the town, or by a firm of certified
public accounts, or by a firm of consulting engineers, which firms shall have
experience in such studies, or by such combination of officers, employees,
certified public accountants or engineers as the town shall determine to be
best under the circumstances.
(Ord. passed - - 2011)
§ 51.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person found to be violating any of the provisions of this
chapter, except §
51.061 of this chapter, shall be served by the town with written notice stating
the nature of the violation and providing a reasonable time limit for the
satisfactory correction thereof. The offender shall, within the period of time
stated in such notice, permanently cease all violations.
   (B)   Any person who shall continue any violation beyond the time limit
provided for in division (A) above shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction thereof, shall be punished in accordance with the provisions of §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (C)   Any person violating any of the forgoing provisions of this chapter
shall become liable to the town for any expense, loss or damage occasioned the
town by reason of such violation.
   (D)   (1)   When the Superintendent finds that a user has violated, or
continues to violate, any provision of §§
51.110 through
51.337, a wastewater discharge permit, or order issued hereunder, or any other
pretreatment standard or requirement, the Superintendent may petition a court
of competent jurisdiction through the Town Attorney for the issuance of a
temporary or permanent injunction, as appropriate, which restrains or compels
the specific performance of the wastewater discharge permit, order, or other
requirement imposed by §§
51.110 through
51.337 on activities of the user. The Superintendent may also seek such other
action as is appropriate for legal and/or equitable relief, including a
requirement for the user to conduct environmental remediation. A petition for
injunctive relief shall not be a bar against, or a prerequisite for, taking any
other action against a user.
      (2)   (a)   A user who has violated, or continues to violate, any
provision of §§
51.110 through
51.337, a wastewater discharge permit, or order issued hereunder, or any other
pretreatment standard or requirement shall be liable to the town for a civil
penalty of not less than $1,000 or more than $2,500 per violation, per day. In
the case of a monthly or other long-term average discharge limit, penalties
shall accrue for each day during the period of the violation.
         (b)   The town may recover reasonable attorney’s fees, court costs and
other expenses associated with enforcement activities, including sampling and
monitoring expenses, and the cost of any actual damages incurred by the town.
         (c)   In determining the amount of civil liability, the court shall
take into account all relevant circumstances, including, but not limited to,
the extent of harm caused by the violation, the magnitude and duration of the
violation, any economic benefit gained through the user’s violation, corrective
actions by the user, the compliance history of the user and any other factors
as justice requires.
         (d)   Filing a suit for civil penalties shall not be a bar against, or
a prerequisite for, taking any other action against a user.
      (3)   Any person who knowingly or willfully makes any false statement,
representation or certification in any application, report or other document
required by §§
51.110 through
51.337 or other regulations adopted by the town, or who tampers with or
knowingly or willfully renders inaccurate any monitoring device so as to render
false information may be subject to I.C. 35-44-2-1. The Town’s Counsel shall,
when appropriate, refer such matters to the County Prosecutor for consideration
of criminal prosecution. The town also reserves the right to refer suspected
knowing or willful violations to the Indiana Department of Environmental
Management or the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, Region 5 for criminal
prosecution. All reports and other documents required to be submitted or
maintained pursuant to §§
51.110 through
51.337 are subject to: the provisions of 18 U.S.C. 1001 relating to fraud and
false statements; provisions of Section 309(c)(4) of the Clean Water Act, as
amended, governing false statements, representations or certification; and the
provisions of Section 309(c)(6) of the Clean Water Act regarding responsible
corporate officers.
      (4)   (a)   The remedies provided for in §§
51.110 through
51.337 are not exclusive. The town may take any, all or any combination of
these actions against a non-compliant user. Enforcement of pretreatment
violations will generally be in accordance with the town’s enforcement response
plan. However, the Superintendent may take other action against any user when
the circumstances warrant. Further, the Superintendent is empowered to take
more than one enforcement action against any non-compliant user. These actions
may be taken concurrently.
         (b)   Any discharger has the right to request in writing an
interpretation or ruling by the town on any matter covered by this §§
51.110 through
51.337 and is entitled to a written reply within 45 days. In the event that
such an inquiry is by the affected discharger and deals with matters of
compliance with §§
51.110 through
51.337 or deals with the permit, receipt of the discharger’s request will delay
all enforcement proceedings, until he receives the written reply.
(1980 Code, § 14-27) (Ord. 1-1977, passed - -1977; Ord. passed - - 2011)
CHAPTER 52: TRASH AND RECYCLABLES
Section
   
52.01   Definitions
   
52.02   Establishment of collection service
   
52.03   Services to be provided
   
52.04   Requirements for pickup
   
52.05   Limitations on trash eligible for collection
   
52.06   Fees
   
52.07   Revenues; special fund
   
52.08   Bulky item collection services
   
52.09   Recyclable collection services
 
   
52.99   Penalty
§ 52.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   RECYCLABLES. Newsprint, magazines, catalogs, aluminum beverage cans, bimetal
and ferrous cans, Category #1 (polyethyl terepthalate) plastic containers,
Category #2 (high density polyethylene) plastic containers and glass bottles
and cardboard.
   TRASH. Ordinary household waste and garbage, including putrescible and non-
putrescible solid and semi-solid items of waste, except for:
      (1)   Human and animal body waste;
      (2)   Liquids;
      (3)   Liquid or water carried wastes which are normally disposed of in
sanitary sewer systems;
      (4)   Hazardous wastes;
      (5)   Building materials;
      (6)   Appliances, furniture, household fixtures and/or any other similar
bulky items;
      (7)   Lead acid batteries; and
      (8)   Yard waste, including, but not limited to, leaves.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.02 ESTABLISHMENT OF COLLECTION SERVICE.
   (A)   The Town Council shall assume the authority for the collection of
residential trash and recyclables, as defined above, and provide a trash and
recyclables collection service solely for the benefit of, and shall be used
solely by, the residents of the town. The Council shall provide the service
through the use of interlocal agreements, contracts, licensing, franchise
arrangements or any method it may deem necessary to ensure that a quality
service is provided to the town’s residents.
   (B)   Prior to awarding any contracts for trash collection under this
chapter, the town shall assure that competitive bids are obtained. Contracts
shall be re-bid periodically to assure competition for providing trash
collection services hereunder. The town reserves the right to award exclusive
franchises for trash collection services for town residents without prior
approval of the Town Council.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.03 SERVICES TO BE PROVIDED.
   The town, acting through the Town Council, and in cooperation with the
Southeastern Indiana Solid Waste District, Versailles, Indiana, shall provide a
curbside or roadside trash collection service to residents occupying housing
units contained in structures with four housing units per structure or less,
small businesses that use curbside service and that lie within the town.
Apartment complexes composed of multiple buildings in a common location and
mobile home courts consisting of multiple units in a common location are not
included. Such trash collection services shall be provided on a designated day
of each week. This schedule, however, is subject to change necessitated by
special circumstances, including, but not limited to, holidays, special events,
severe weather or equipment breakdown. Any changes in the above schedule will
be announced by the town, in advance if possible, through the local news media.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.04 REQUIREMENTS FOR PICKUP.
   (A)   Items which are to be picked up pursuant to this trash collection
service shall be placed adjacent to the curb immediately adjacent to the
individual’s residence, within three feet of the curb or traveled portion of
the street prior to 6:00 a.m. on the date scheduled for said collection, but
not earlier than 6:00 p.m. on the date preceding the scheduled collection.
   (B)   No person shall place any trash on the property of another. No person
shall search through, alter, deform or remove any items set out for collection.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.05 LIMITATIONS ON TRASH ELIGIBLE FOR COLLECTION.
   No person shall dispose of, for collection, any needles, syringes, lancets
or other sharp objects, unless the same are placed in a hard plastic or metal
container with a screw-on or tightly-secured lid. No person shall dispose of,
for collection, any soiled bandages, disposable sheets or medical gloves,
unless the same are placed in a securely fastened plastic bag.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.06 FEES.
   (A)   For purposes of this section, the term ELIGIBLE RESIDENTIAL DWELLING
UNIT means a structure located within the town which includes a room or series
of rooms located within a building or mobile home and forming a single
habitable unit with facilities, which are used, or are intended to be used for
living, cooking, eating and sleeping, and are either:
      (1)   A SINGLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL DWELLING UNIT, defined as a dwelling
unit separated from any other dwelling unit by open space, and designed for
occupancy for one person or family;
      (2)   A MULTI-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL DWELLING UNIT, defined as a building or
related group of buildings not to exceed four units located on the same lot,
tract, or parcel of real estate, with each dwelling unit being independent of
the other; or
      (3)   A CONDOMINIUM, defined as any multi-family residential unit where
the owner pays separately assessed property taxes.
   (B)   There is hereby imposed a trash collection fee as follows:
      (1)   Beginning with the first bill after the adoption of Ordinance 2025-
3 for eligible residential dwelling units payable through bills payable in
November of 2025, the imposed trash and recycling collection fee shall be
$16.75, plus $0.25 for composting;
      (2)   Beginning with the bill payable in December of 2025 through bills
payable in November of 2026, the imposed trash and recycling collection fee
shall be $17.51, plus $0.26 for composting; and
      (3)   Beginning with the bill payable in December of 2026 through bills
payable in November of 2027, the imposed trash and recycling collection fee
shall be $18.29, plus $0.27 for composting.
   (C)   The rate imposed by this section shall be in addition to the rates
charged by the town to operate its municipal sewage works and any other rates
for the collection and treatment of wastewater by the town's wastewater
utility.
   (D)   All unpaid fees imposed by this section are subject to the same
delinquency fees, penalties, and interest authorized for delinquent sewer bills
and may be collected by any lawful means including the filing of liens as
authorized by I.C. 36-9-23.
   (E)   The rates approved by this section shall be in full force and
effective for all wastewater bills due after February 19, 2025, and thereafter.
   (F)   In the event a resident will be leaving his or her residence
unoccupied by any person for a continuous period of not less than 90 days and,
as a result, shall not require pickup, such resident may notify the town and
during such period shall be entitled to an abatement of the fees normally
charged under this section. The resident must, within five days of the
reoccupancy of his or her residence, after a period of suspension of charges
under this section, notify the town of the date of reoccupancy and charges and
pickup shall resume under the normal fee and pickup schedule.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003; Ord. 6-2007, passed 10-14-2003; Ord. 2017-1,
passed 2-14-2017; Ord. 2025-3, passed 1-21-2025)
§ 52.07 REVENUES; SPECIAL FUND.
   The revenue generated from the collection of service charges from trash
collection as provided for above shall be deposited in a separate fund and
account from any other fund established and funded by tax monies, expenditures
from which shall be limited to costs associated with the collection and
disposal of said trash and the purchase of equipment necessary to provide such
services to the residents and citizens of the community.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.08 BULKY ITEM COLLECTION SERVICES.
   All normal, large household items such as furniture, stoves, water heaters
and other appliances will be acceptable for this collection. Refrigerators, air
conditioners or other items which normally contain CFCs (Freon) will not be
collected unless documentation is provided that the CFCs have been properly
removed. No hazardous materials, lead acid batteries, tires or any other items
prohibited by federal, state, county or local laws or regulations will be
accepted. Bulky items, excluded from the definition of trash, will be collected
four times a year on dates scheduled by the Town Council.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.09 RECYCLABLE COLLECTION SERVICES.
   As part of the services provided by the town, recyclable items including
newsprint, aluminum beverage cans, bi-metal and ferrous cans, Category #1
(polyethyl terepthalate) plastic containers, Category #2 (high density
polyethylene) plastic containers, magazines, catalogs, cardboard and glass
bottles shall be collected twice monthly on days to be designated by the Town
Council. Recyclables need not be separated. A recycling bin will be provided.
Recyclable items shall contain no free flowing liquids, but need not be free of
labels. Recyclable beverage containers shall have cap and neck rings removed.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)
§ 52.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person, firm or corporation violating any of the provisions of
this chapter shall be guilty of a municipal ordinance violation, punishable by
a municipal fine up to $100 for the first offense, up to $500 for a second
offense occurring during the same calendar year and $1,000 for each subsequent
offense occurring during the same calendar year.
   (B)   In addition to the fines imposed by division (A) above for the
violation of any provision of this chapter, the householder shall also be
liable to the town for all damages and expenses incurred by the town by reason
of such violation in a civil action brought for such purpose.
(Ord. 3-2003, passed 10-14-2003)

*** GENERAL PROVISIONS ***
§ 70.001 SCOPE.
   The provisions of this chapter shall govern and regulate the use, standing
or parking upon streets and sidewalks; as to motor vehicles generally and to
noises generally as to streets and sidewalks.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
§ 70.002 AUTHORITY.
   The Town Council shall place and maintain traffic-control, parking signs,
signals and devices when, and as required under this chapter and other traffic
ordinances of the town, to make effective such provisions, and may place and
maintain such additional traffic-control devices as it may deem necessary to
regulate traffic under this chapter, other traffic ordinances of the town or
under state law or to guide or warn traffic.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
§ 70.003 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      PARK. The standing of a vehicle, whether occupied or not, otherwise than
temporarily for the purpose of and while actually engaged in loading or
unloading.
      STANDING. Any stopping of a vehicle, whether occupied or not.
      STOP. When required, complete cessation of movement. When prohibited, any
stopping of a vehicle, except when necessary to avoid conflict with other
traffic or in compliance with the direction of a police officer, traffic
officer or traffic-control sign or signal.
   (B)   Whenever any words and phrases used herein are not defined in any
section of this code or other ordinances of the town, but are defined in the
state’s vehicle laws, such definitions shall be deemed to apply to such words
and phrases used herein.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
§ 70.004 COMPLIANCE.
   It is unlawful for a person to do any act forbidden by this chapter or to
fail to perform any act required in this chapter and said person shall be
subject to the penalty provisions of the Indiana Code and/or this chapter
including, but not necessarily limited to, §
70.999.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009; Ord. 2024-4, passed 3-4-2024)
§ 70.005 ROLLER SKATES, SKATE BOARDS, TOY VEHICLES AND THE LIKE; DRIVING IN
PARK.
   (A)   No person upon roller skates, a skate board or riding in or by means
of any coaster, toy vehicle or similar device shall go upon any roadway, except
while crossing a street or sidewalk, except upon streets set aside as play
streets.
   (B)   No two- or three-wheel motorized vehicles, nor any motorized bicycles,
nor any four-wheel unlicensed motorized vehicles shall be operated in the
Brookville Community Park.
(Ord. 1-1985, passed 10-22-1985; Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
*** STOPPING, STANDING AND PARKING ***
§ 70.020 FAILURE TO LEAVE ADEQUATE SPACE.
   No person shall stop, stand or park any vehicle upon a street, other than an
alley, in such a manner or under such conditions as to leave available less
than ten feet of the width of the roadway for free movement of vehicular
traffic; except that, a driver may stop temporarily during the actual loading
or unloading of passengers or when necessary in obedience to traffic
regulations or traffic signs, signals or a police officer. No person shall park
a vehicle within an alley in such a manner or under such conditions as to leave
available less than eight feet of width of the roadway for the free movement of
vehicular traffic.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.021 PASSENGER ZONES; LOADING/UNLOADING ZONES.
   No person shall stand, stop or park a vehicle for any purpose or period of
time other than for the expeditious loading or unloading of passengers in any
place marked as a passenger zone during hours when the regulations applicable
to such passenger zone are effective, and then only for a period not to exceed
three minutes. No person shall stop, stand or park a vehicle for any purpose or
length of time other than the expeditious unloading and delivery or picking up
and loading of materials in any place marked as a loading zone during hours
when the provisions applicable to loading zones are in effect. In no case shall
the stop for loading and unloading of materials exceed 30 minutes.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.022 TAXICABS; TAXICAB STANDS.
   The driver of a taxicab shall not stand or park upon any street in the
business district at any place other than a taxicab stand; except that, this
provision shall not prevent the driver of such vehicle from temporarily
stopping in accordance with other stopping or parking regulations at any place
for the purpose of and while actually engaged in loading or unloading
passengers. No person shall stop, stand or park a vehicle, other than a
taxicab, in a taxicab stand when any such stop or stand has been officially
designated and appropriately signed; except that, the driver of a passenger
vehicle may temporarily stop therein for a purpose of and while actually
engaged in loading and unloading passengers when such stopping does not
interfere with any taxicab waiting to enter or about to enter such zone.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.023 METHOD OF PARKING.
   No person shall stand or park a vehicle on a roadway other than parallel
with the edge of the roadway, headed in the direction of traffic, and with the
curb-side wheels of the vehicle within 12 inches of the edge of the roadway;
except that, upon those streets which have been marked or signed for angle
parking, vehicles shall be parked at the angle to the curb indicated by such
marks or signs. No vehicle shall be parked in a business district, where
parking is flat to the curb, where such vehicle will not fit into the marked
parking space for one vehicle; and where angle parking is designated, no
vehicle shall be parked in a parking space so marked that is longer than the
average passenger vehicle, unless such parking be only for the purpose of
loading or unloading of passengers or cargo.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.024 DISPLAY FOR SALE; ADVERTISING; WASHING, GREASING OR REPAIRING.
   No person shall stand or park a vehicle upon any street or roadway for the
principal purpose of displaying it for sale or for advertising or promoting any
type of business, or for washing, greasing or repairing such vehicle, except
repairs necessitated by an emergency.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.025 PARKING IN CERTAIN AREAS; MOVEMENT OF VEHICLE INTO PROHIBITED
LOCATION.
   (A)   No person shall stop, stand or park a vehicle, except when necessary
to avoid conflict with other traffic or in compliance with the directions of a
police officer or traffic-control device, in any of the following places:
      (1)   A sidewalk;
      (2)   In front of a private drive;
      (3)   Within an intersection;
      (4)   Within 15 feet of a fire hydrant;
      (5)   On a crosswalk;
      (6)   Within three feet of a crosswalk or an intersection;
      (7)   Along or opposite any street excavation or obstruction when such
stopping, standing or parking would obstruct traffic;
      (8)   On the roadway side of any vehicle stopped or parked at the edge or
curb of a street; and
      (9)   At any place where official signs prohibit stopping.
   (B)   No person shall move a vehicle not owned by such person into any such
prohibited area or away from the curb such distance as is unlawful.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.026 PARKING OF RESTRICTED VEHICLES.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this section is to educate the public and
promote public health and welfare relating to the parking of restricted
vehicles on the street within the incorporated limits of the town.
   (B)   Definitions. 
      (1)   When used in this section, the following words, terms and phrases,
and their derivations shall have the meanings ascribed to them in this section,
except where the context clearly indicates a different meaning. When not
inconsistent with the context, words used in the present tense include the
future, words used in the plural number include the singular number, and words
used in the singular number include the plural number. The word “shall” is
always mandatory and not merely discretionary.
      (2)   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall
apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
         RESTRICTED VEHICLES. Any of the following; includes but not limited
to:
            1,   COMMERCIAL VEHICLES. Semis/ Semi-trailers, buses, wreckers,
box trucks/ vans, mobile homes, stretch limos, tree removal trucks/ chippers.
            2.   FARM EQUIPMENT. Tractors, combines, and agricultural wagons.
            3.   RECREATIONAL VEHICLES/ITEMS. Boats, jet skis, all-terrain
vehicles, campers, (motorized or not) golf carts, race/demolition-cars, play
equipment.
            4.   CONSTRUCTION AND OTHER EQUIPMENT. Backhoes, air compressors,
dump trucks, bucket lifts, hauling trailers of all sizes.
   (C)   Prohibited parking. No person shall park any restricted vehicle on any
street in the incorporated limits of the town except in the following
circumstances:
      (1)   While in performance of official duties such as loading or
unloading, tree trimming/chipping trees, permitted construction work.
      (2)   While in performance of the above-mentioned duties restricted
vehicles must be kept in front of property boundaries in which said duties are
being performed unless permission is granted from abutting land owners.
      (3)   Penalty. If after a verbal warning said owner does not remove the
restricted vehicle within three days then a written warning will be given to
remove the vehicle with an additional three days notice. If the restricted
vehicle is not removed then the owner will be fined in accordance with §
70.999.
(Ord. 2016-3, passed 6-14-2016)
§ 70.027 RESERVED.
§ 70.028 HAZARDOUS OR CONGESTED PLACES.
   When signs are erected upon approach to hazardous or congested places, no
person shall stop, stand or park a vehicle in any such designated place.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.029 TOWING AND IMPOUNDING OF VEHICLES.
   In all violations of any section of this subchapter wherein unlawful parking
is involved, the vehicle may be removed or towed away at the expense of the
owner or operator of such vehicle; and shall remain impounded until all fines
and costs, including towing expenses shall have been paid in full.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
*** METERED PARKING ***
§ 70.040 PARKING WITHIN LINES OR MARKINGS REQUIRED.
   (A)   Each vehicle being parked adjacent to a parking meter shall be parked
within the lines or markings painted or placed on the curb and or upon the
street adjacent to each parking meter for the purpose of designating the
parking space for which said meter is to be used.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful to park any vehicle across any such line or
marking or to park such vehicle is such position that the same shall not be
entirely within the area so designed by such lines or markings.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.041 INSTRUCTIONS AND INDICATIONS OF METERS.
   Parking meters in metered parking zones have been set in such a manner as to
show or display by a signal that the parking space adjacent to such meter is or
is not legally in use. Each parking meter indicates by a proper legend the
legal parking time heretofore established by the town and, when operated as
indicated, the duration of the period of legal parking and, on the expiration
of such period, illegal or over parking.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
§ 70.042 PARKING IN PARALLEL PARKING SPACES.
   When a parking space in any parking meter zone is parallel with adjacent
curb or sidewalk, any vehicle parked in such parking space shall be parked with
the foremost part of such vehicle nearest to such meter.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.043 OPERATION OF METERS; LIMITATIONS OF USE OF SPACE.
   (A)   (1)   When a vehicle is parked in any space, adjacent to which a
parking meter is located in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter,
the operator of such vehicle shall, upon entering such parking space,
immediately deposit or cause to be deposited a $0.25 coin in such parking meter
and put such meter in operation.
      (2)   Failure to deposit such coin and put the meter in operation shall
constitute a violation of this subchapter and shall subject such person to
penalty prescribed in this code.
   (B)   (1)   Upon the deposit of such coin and placing the meter in
operation, the parking space may be lawfully occupied by such vehicle during
the period of parking time which has been prescribed for the part of the street
in which such parking space is located.
      (2)   If such vehicle shall remain parked in any such parking space
beyond the parking time limit fixed for such parking space and the parking
meter indicates, by its dial and pointer, such illegal parking, such vehicle
shall be considered as parked overtime and beyond the period of legal parking
time.
   (C)   The parking of a vehicle overtime or beyond the period of legal
parking time in any such part of a street where any such meter is located shall
be a violation of this subchapter and punished as herein after set out.
   (D)   Commercial trucks may park in such parking zone for the period not
exceeding 30 minutes, to load or unload merchandise without depositing such
coins. Should any truck or delivery car be parked longer than 30 minutes, the
violator will be given a ticket and classed as a violator and the regular
penalty will be imposed.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.044 OVERTIME PARKING.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to cause, allow, permit or suffer any
vehicle registered in the name of or operated by such person to be parked
overtime or beyond the period of legal parking time established for any parking
meter zone as herein prescribed.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.045 PARKING BEYOND PERMITTED TIME PERIOD.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to permit any vehicle to remain or to be
placed in any parking space adjacent to any parking meter while such meter is
displayed a signal indicating that the vehicle occupying such parking space has
already been parked beyond the period of time prescribed for such parking
space.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.046 DEFACING OR DESTROYING METERS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to deface, injure, tamper with, open,
willfully break, destroy or impair the usefulness of any parking meter.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.047 USE OF SLUGS AND THE LIKE.
   It shall be unlawful to deposit or cause to be deposited in any parking
meter in town any slug or slugs, device or metallic substance or any substitute
for the coins herein above required.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.048 REPORTS OF POLICE TO COUNCIL; NOTICES OF VIOLATIONS; PAYMENT OF FINES.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the police officers of the town, acting in
accordance with instructions issued by the Town Board, to report:
      (1)   The number of each parking meter which indicates that the vehicle
occupying the parking space adjacent to such parking meter is or has been
parking in violation of any of the provisions of this subchapter;
      (2)   The state license number of such vehicle;
      (3)   The time during which such vehicle is parking in violation of any
of the provisions of this subchapter;
      (4)   Any other facts, or knowledge of which is necessary to a thorough
understanding of the circumstances attending such violation.
   (B)   (1)   Each such police officer shall also attach to such vehicle a
notice to the owner or operator thereof that such vehicle has been parked in
violation of the provisions of this subchapter.
      (2)   Each such owner or operator shall within 30 days of the time when
such notice was attached to such vehicle, pay to the Brookville Police
Department, place in a fine-drop box attached to the front of the Brookville
Police Department, or mailing payment to the Brookville Police Department at
634 Main Street, Brookville, Indiana 47012, for the purpose of such collection
as a penalty for and in full satisfaction of such violation, the sum of $20.
      (3)   If penalty is paid within ten days from the date of citation, the
penalty is reduced to $10. After ten days and up to 30 days the penalty is $20.
After 30 days the fine is doubled to $40.
      (4)   Fines for parking violations other than parking meters:
         (a)   Blocking an alley: $20.
         (b)   Yellow curb: $20.
         (c)   Fire Hydrant: $20.
         (d)   Improper parking: $20.
         (e)   Handicap Zone: $25.
      (5)   The failure of such owner or operator to make such payment shall
render such owner or operator subject to the penalties hereinafter provided for
violation of any of the provisions of this subchapter and will be collected in
any manner authorized by law.
      (6)   Appeals process. If you believe your citation was issued in error,
you may appeal, in writing, to the Town Clerk within ten days from the date
citation was issued. An appeals form can be obtained at the Municipal Building
at 1020 Franklin Ave., Brookville, Indiana 47012 or the form may also be
printed off the website and sent to the address above. Website:
www.brookvilleindiana.org.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009; Ord. 2018-1, passed 5-9-2018; Ord. 2021-12,
passed 5-25-2021) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.049 PURPOSE OF FEES.
   The specified coins required to be deposited in parking meters as provided
herein are hereby levied and assessed as fees:
   (A)   To provide for the proper regulation and control of traffic upon the
public streets;
   (B)   To provide for the cost of supervision and regulation of the parking
of vehicles in parking meter zones; and
   (C)   To cover the cost of operation, maintenance, supervision, regulation
and control of the parking meters described herein.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009; Ord. 2021-12, passed 5-25-2021)
§ 70.050 REMOVAL OF VEHICLES PARKED IN EXCESS OF 24 HOURS.
   Any vehicle that is parked at a meter for 24 hours continuously may be
removed or towed away at the expense of the owner or operator of such vehicle,
and shall remain impounded until all fines and costs, including towing
expenses, shall have been paid in full.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009)
§ 70.051 TOWING AND IMPOUNDMENT OF VEHICLES WITH FIVE OR MORE VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   Any vehicle that is parked in violation of any section or provision of
this subchapter may be removed or towed away at the expense of the owner or
operator of such vehicle when it has five or more outstanding unpaid
violations.
   (B)   Such vehicle shall remain impounded until all fines and costs,
including towing expenses, shall have been paid in full.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009; Ord. 2021-12, passed 5-25-2021)
*** VEHICLE OPERATION ***
§ 70.065 QUIET ZONES.
   Whenever authorized signs are erected indicating a zone of quiet, no person
operating a motor vehicle within any such zone shall sound the horn or other
warning device of such vehicle, except in an emergency.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.066 PLAY STREETS.
   Whenever authorized signs are erected indicating any street or part thereof
as a play street, no person shall drive a vehicle upon such street or portion
thereof, except persons having business or whose residences are within such
closed areas. Any person driving on such streets shall exercise the greatest
care.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.067 SPEED LIMITS.
   (A)   No person shall drive a vehicle on a street or roadway of the town at
a speed greater than is reasonable and prudent under the conditions then
existing.
   (B)   Where no special hazard exists, the following speeds shall be lawful,
but any speed in excess of such limits shall be prima facie evidence that the
speed is not reasonable or prudent and is unlawful:
      (1)   Twenty mph in any business district; and
      (2)   Twenty-five mph in any residence district.
   (C)   The fact that the speed of a vehicle is lower than the foregoing prima
facie limits shall not relieve the driver from the duty to decease speed when
special hazards exist with respect to pedestrians or other traffic or by reason
of weather or highway conditions; and speed shall be decreased as may be
necessary to avoid colliding with any person, vehicle or other conveyance on or
entering the highway in compliance with legal requirements and the duty of all
persons to use due care.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.068 DRIVING ON SIDEWALKS.
   The driver of a vehicle shall not drive within any sidewalk area, except at
a permanent or temporary driveway.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.069 BACKING.
   The driver of a vehicle shall not back the same into an intersection or over
a crosswalk and shall not, in any event or any place, back a vehicle unless
such movement can be made in safety.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.070 TURNING IN STREETS.
   The driver of any vehicle shall not turn such vehicle so as to proceed in
the opposite direction upon any street in a business district, and shall not,
upon any other street outside of a business district, so turn a vehicle unless
such movement can be made in safety and without interfering with other traffic.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009) Penalty, see §
70.999
§ 70.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code.
   (B)   Any person violating the provisions of §
70.005(B) shall, upon conviction thereof, be punished by a fine not to exceed
$100, together with court costs.
   (C)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
70.040 through
70.051 and any person who aids, abets, or assists therein shall be prosecuted
as provided in §
70.048(B).
   (D)   Any person found to be in violation of any schedule found in
Chapter 72 shall be penalized as follows:
      (1)   Any person found to be in violation of any schedule found in
Chapter 72 shall be issued a written warning by the Police Department for their
first violation of said schedule.
      (2)   A second violation of any schedule found in
Chapter 72 in a 12-month period shall be a Class D infraction and the person
shall be issued an ordinance violation ticket by the Police Department
punishable by a fine of up to $25.
      (3)   A third and any subsequent violations of any schedule found in
Chapter 72 in a 12-month period shall be a Class C infraction and the person
shall be issued an ordinance violation ticket by the Police Department
punishable by a fine of up to $500.
   (E)   In addition to the foregoing penalties, any person found to be in
violation of any section of
Chapter 70 and/or
Chapter 72 shall be subject to the towing and impoundment provisions found in §
70.029.
(Ord. 2-2009, passed 5-12-2009; Ord. 2024-4, passed 3-4-2024)
CHAPTER 71: RECREATIONAL OFF-ROAD VEHICLES
AND GOLF CARTS
Section
General Provisions
   
71.01   General regulations
   
71.02   Inspection of recreational off-road vehicles
Golf Carts
   
71.15   Purpose
   
71.16   Definitions
   
71.17   Rules and regulations generally
   
71.18   Inspections and fees
   
71.19   Enforcement
 
   
71.99   Penalty
*** GENERAL PROVISIONS ***
§ 71.01 GENERAL REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Subject to the regulations contained in this chapter, only
recreational off-road vehicles as defined by I.C. 14-8-2-233.5 are hereby
allowed on the streets of the Town of Brookville subject to the conditions set
forth in this chapter. More specifically, neither golf carts nor all-terrain
vehicles, as defined by I.C. 14-8-2-5.7, are permitted by this chapter.
   (B)   Recreational off-road vehicles must have proof of a valid liability
insurance covering the vehicle in an amount greater to or equal to the Indiana
state automotive insurance minimums as set forth in I.C. 9-25-4-5 when operated
on Town of Brookville streets.
   (C)   In order to qualify under this chapter, a recreational off-road
vehicle must have a rollover protection system installed. Furthermore, the
recreational off-road vehicle must be equipped with seatbelts for each
passenger including the driver.
   (D)   Recreational off-road vehicle operators on Town of Brookville streets
must be at least 16 years of age or older and hold a valid driver's license.
   (E)   No passengers are permitted on single seat recreational off-road
vehicles on town streets. All other vehicles subject to this chapter are
limited to the number of passenger seats on the vehicle.
   (F)   Recreational off-road vehicles must have at least one operating brake
light when in operation on town streets.
   (G)   No recreational off-road vehicles are permitted on town roadways from
sunset to sunrise unless they have functioning efficient lights as per state
law.
   (H)   Recreational off-road vehicles must travel single file on town
streets.
   (I)   Failure to abide by any of the above conditions when recreational off-
road vehicles are in use on town streets and roadways shall constitute a
violation of this chapter.
   (J)   Recreational off-road vehicles shall not be operated on private
property without the permission and consent of the property owner.
   (K)   Recreational off-road vehicles may not operate on streets or highways
under the jurisdiction of the State Department of Transportation. Recreational
off-road vehicles may cross these streets or highways to access other streets
at intersections only. Crossing must be made at a right angle. Residents
traveling from areas only connected by a state highway may travel along these
highways to access the nearest street. The specific streets and highways
referenced include Fourth Street or State Road 252, Main Street or U.S. Highway
52 and State Road 101.
   (L)   Recreational off-road vehicles may be operated on Mill Street and
Fairfield Avenue except for the stretch of roadway between the intersections of
9th and 10th Street (Ball Park Hill). Traveling in the roadway between these
intersections will constitute a violation of this chapter.
   (M)   Recreation off-road vehicles may not operation sidewalks.
   (N)   Recreational off-road vehicles shall not be operated on town streets
and roadways with overhanging cargo. Any items in the loading areas of the
recreational off-road vehicle extending more than one foot from the vehicle
shall be marked with an orange or red caution flag.
   (O)   Operators of recreational off-road vehicles are required to remove a
wrecked or damaged recreational off-road vehicle and any and all glass or other
foreign material dropped onto a roadway in the event of an accident.
(Ord. 2020-15, passed 10-13-2020; Ord. 2021-16, passed 7-27-2021)
§ 71.02 INSPECTION OF RECREATIONAL OFF-ROAD VEHICLES.
   The inspection by the Chief of Police, or his or her designee, will cover
the following safety requirements and every recreational off-road vehicle
operating on town streets must have the following safety equipment:
   (A)   Permits/stickers issued to operators/owners of recreational off-road
vehicles by the town Police Department;
   (B)   Each owner must have proof of ownership, and liability insurance and a
completed hold harmless agreement releasing the town, its employees and
affiliates from all liability that may arise as a result of operating a
recreational off-road vehicle inside the town. A current hold harmless
agreement must be on file with the town Police Department, and must be renewed
annually;
   (C)   All recreational off-road vehicles must meet the requirements or
minimum standards of safety equipment as set forth in this chapter;
   (D)   All recreational off-road vehicles operators must present a valid
driver's license;
   (E)   Permits/stickers will be issued annually and are valid from the date
issued until the same time the following year and displayed on the windshield
of the recreational off-road vehicle:
      (1)   Registration and inspection by Police Department:
         (a)   Incorporated Town of Brookville residents: $50 annually
(includes permit/sticker); and
         (b)   Non-residents of the town: $75 annually (includes permit/
sticker).
      (2)   Re-inspection by Police Department: $25 (if a recreational off-road
vehicle fails the initial inspection).
   (F)   Lost or stolen permit/stickers are the responsibility of the owner. A
police report must be filed in the event of a lost or stolen permit/sticker.
The Chief of Police will have the discretion in determining whether a permit/
sticker may be re-issued in this instance. If no record can be found on a
previous application, or the receipt of a permit/sticker, the Chief of Police
may direct the applicant to reapply, and also resubmit any and all fees
necessary, before a replacement permit/sticker is issued;
   (G)   Any person who operates a recreational off-road vehicle in the town
and fails to obtain and properly display a town or police permit/sticker on the
left lower side of the windshield will be subject to all applicable state laws,
in addition to being in violation of this chapter; and
   (H)   Recreational off-road vehicle owners must complete the registration
form attached to Ordinance 1-2010, hold harmless agreement form and provide a
copy of the proof of liability insurance prior to the recreational off-road
vehicle being inspected. The completed forms and proof of insurance will be on
file and maintained by the town Police Department.
(Ord. 2020-15, passed 10-13-2020; Ord. 2021-16, passed 7-27-2021)
*** GOLF CARTS ***
§ 71.15 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this chapter shall be to establish a golf cart ordinance
within the town to promote the health, safety and welfare of persons operating
cart(s) within the town.
(Ord. 1-2010, passed 1-26-2010)
§ 71.16 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DRIVER’S LICENSE. A valid license issued to operate a motor vehicle issued
by Indiana or any other state.
   FINANCIAL RESPONSIBILITY. Liability insurance coverage on a golf cart in an
amount not less than required by state law for motor vehicles operated on
public highway in the state.
   GOLF CART. A vehicle designed and manufactured for operation on a golf
course for sporting or recreational purposes and that is not capable of
exceeding speeds of 25 mph. ATVs are not permitted.
   OPERATOR. Only persons over 16 years of age and holding a driver’s license
may operate a golf cart.
(Ord. 1-2010, passed 1-26-2010)
§ 71.17 RULES AND REGULATIONS GENERALLY.
   (A)   Carts may be driven on roads only if equipped with two operating
headlights (one on each side of the front of the golf cart) and two operating
taillights with brake lights (one on each side of the rear of the cart), which
are visible from a distance of 500 feet, turn signals front and rear,
windshield and seat belts. No one under two years of age shall be permitted to
ride in the golf cart. The driver and all passengers must wear seat belts.
   (B)   Carts must be equipped with a rear vision mirror and either a slow-
moving vehicle emblem or a flashing red or amber light to the rear.
   (C)   Cart drivers must have a valid driver’s license in their name.
   (D)   Cart drivers will stay to the far right of the traveled portion of the
road and yield the right-of-way to overtaking vehicles.
   (E)   (1)   The number of occupants in a golf cart shall be limited to the
number of persons for whom individual seating is installed and provided on the
golf cart.
      (2)   The operator and all occupants shall be seated in the golf cart and
no part of the body of the operator or occupant shall extend outside the
perimeter of the golf cart while the golf cart is in motion.
   (F)   All applicable state laws shall be adhered to, including the
possession and use of alcoholic beverages.
   (G)   The operator of the golf cart shall comply with all traffic rules and
regulations adopted by the state and town which governs the operation of motor
vehicles.
   (H)   Carts will adhere to all traffic flow patterns and will operate on the
right side of the roadway.
   (I)   Golf carts shall not be operated on sidewalks. Golf carts may be
operated on Mill Street and Fairfield Avenue, except for the stretch of roadway
between the intersections of 9th and 10th Street (Ball Park Hill). Traveling in
the roadway between these intersections will constitute a violation of this
section.
   (J)   Carts shall not be operated on private property without the permission
and consent of the property owner.
   (K)   Motorized carts may not operate on streets or highways under the
jurisdiction of the State Department of Transportation. Motorized carts may
cross these streets or highway to access other streets at intersections only.
Crossing must be made at a right angle. The specific streets and highways
referenced include Fourth Street or State Road 252, Main Street or U.S. Highway
52 and State Road 101.
   (L)   Operators of carts are required to remove a wrecked or damaged golf
cart and any and all glass or other foreign material dropped onto a roadway in
the event of an accident.
(Ord. 1-2010, passed 1-26-2010; Ord. 2021-17, passed 7-27-2021) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.18 INSPECTIONS AND FEES.
   The inspection by the Chief of Police, or his or her designee, will cover
the following safety requirements and every cart operating on town streets must
have the following safety equipment.
   (A)   Permits/stickers issued to operators/owners of golf carts by the town
Police Department.
   (B)   Each owner must have proof of ownership, liability insurance and a
completed hold harmless agreement, releasing the town, its employees and
affiliates from all liability that may arise as a result of operating a cart
inside the town. A current hold harmless agreement must be on file with the
town Police Department, and must be renewed annually.
   (C)   All carts must meet the requirements or minimum standards of safety
equipment as set forth in § 71.03 of this chapter.
   (D)   All cart operators must present a valid driver’s license.
   (E)   Permits/stickers will be issued annually and are valid from the date
issued until the same time the following year and displayed on the windshield
of the golf cart:
      (1)   Registration and inspection by Police Department:
         (a)   Residents: $50 annually (includes permit/sticker); and
         (b)   Non-residents: $75 annually (includes permit/sticker).
      (2)   Re-inspection by Police Department: $25 (if a cart fails the
initial inspection).
   (F)   Lost or stolen permit/stickers are the responsibility of the owner. A
police report must be filed in the event of a lost or stolen permit/sticker.
The Chief of Police will have the discretion in determining whether a permit/
sticker may be re-issued in this instance. If no record can be found of a
previous application, or the receipt of a permit/sticker, the Chief of Police
may direct the applicant to reapply, and also resubmit any and all fees
necessary, before a replacement permit/sticker is issued.
   (G)   Any person who operates a cart in the town and fails to receive and
properly display a town or police permit/sticker on the left lower side of the
windshield will be subject to all applicable state laws, in addition to being
in violation of this chapter.
   (H)   Cart owners must complete the registration form attached to Ordinance
1-2010, hold harmless agreement form and provide a copy of the proof of
liability insurance prior to the cart being inspected. The completed forms and
proof of insurance will be maintained by the town Police Department.
(Ord. 1-2010, passed 1-26-2010; Ord. 2020-16, passed 11-10-2020) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.19 ENFORCEMENT.
   It shall be the policy of the town Police Department to issue a uniform
citation against any person the officer has probable cause to believe has
violated this chapter, whether the offending person is a juvenile or any other
person.
(Ord. 1-2010, passed 1-26-2010)
§ 71.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Fines and penalties for violations of this chapter.
      (1)   First violation: $50.
      (2)   Second violation: $100 and a 60 day suspension from using
recreational off-road vehicles on town streets.
      (3)   Third and subsequent violations: $250 and a lifetime suspension
from using recreational off-road vehicles on town streets.
      (4)   The violation of a lifetime suspension shall result in a $500 fine
for each offense.
      (5)   Fines may be paid through the Town of Brookville Clerk-Treasurer or
may be contested to the Town of Brookville Chief of Police and Town
Administrator and if requested may be heard by the Town Council.
      (6)   All fines shall be deposited in the Town of Brookville General
Fund.
   (B)   Enforcement of this chapter shall be conducted pursuant to I.C. 34-28-
5.
(Ord. 2020-15, passed 10-13-2020; Ord. 2020-16, passed 10-13-2020; Ord. 2021-
16, passed 7-27-2021)
CHAPTER 72: PARKING SCHEDULES

§ 72.01 SCOPE, AUTHORITY, AND VIOLATION.
   (A)   The following parking schedules shall apply within the town as
indicated in each parking schedule. Violations of these parking schedules are
subject to, but are not necessarily limited to, the penalties described in §
70.999(D).
   (B)   Parking schedules may be modified by the Town Council, on a temporary
basis, by the adoption of a resolution by the Town Council making such
modification and the length of time for said modification. Violations of any
temporarily modified parking schedule are subject to, but are not necessarily
limited to, the penalties described in §
70.999(D).
(Ord. 2024-11, passed 6-17-2024)

park a vehicle in any such designated place. When signs are erected indicating
no parking upon that side of the street, no person shall park a vehicle in any
such designated place. Violations of this schedule shall be subject to the
penalties outlined in §
70.999(D) of this code. The streets and alleys hereinafter described, when
properly marked and posted with appropriate signs, shall hereafter be no
parking areas, and no person shall, at any time, park a vehicle upon any of the
following described streets or alleys or parts thereof.

§ 90.01 CONSTRUCTION, MAINTENANCE AND THE LIKE OF SIDEWALKS, CURBS AND GUTTERS;
OWNER TO BE RESPONSIBLE.
   (A)   The owner of every lot or parcel of ground within the town shall be
solely responsible for the construction, maintenance and repair of the
sidewalks, curbs and gutters abutting such lot or parcel of ground, whether
such sidewalk, curb or gutter are already constructed or are hereafter
constructed.
   (B)   In the event of any injury or damage caused by reason of the
negligence of such property owner in the keeping and maintaining of such
sidewalk, curb and gutter in proper maintenance and repair, such injury or
damage shall be the sole liability of such abutting property owner.
   (C)   All sidewalks, curbs and gutters shall be constructed, maintained and
repaired in accordance with the laws of the state.
(1980 Code, § 13-1) (Ord. 2-1970, passed - -; Ord. 3-1970, passed - -)
§ 90.02 RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNER, OCCUPANT AND THE LIKE OF PROPERTIES TO KEEP
SIDEWALKS FREE OF SNOW, ICE, DIRT OR OTHER OBSTRUCTION.
   (A)   Every owner or occupant of any house or other building, and the owner
or proprietor, lessee or persons entitled to possession of any vacant lot, and
every person having charge of any church, jail, public hall or other public
building in the town shall, during the winter season and during the time snow
or freezing rain shall continue on the ground, by 9:00 a.m. on each and every
day, clean the sidewalk in front of such house or building and in front of such
lot, from snow or ice, keeping it conveniently free thereof during the day.
   (B)   Every such person shall, at all times, keep the sidewalk abutting such
property clean from all dirt or filth or other obstruction or encumbrance, so
as to allow citizens to use such sidewalk in an easy and commodious manner.
(1980 Code, § 13-2) (Ord. 4-1970, passed - -) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.03 OBSTRUCTIONS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to place, suffer to be placed or permit
any boxes containing merchandise of any kind and description of property
whatever to be placed in or upon any of the streets, sidewalks or alleys of the
town and allow them to remain in or on such streets, sidewalks or alleys for
any greater length of time than with due diligence they may be removed for the
purposes for which they are to be used; except that, this section shall not
apply to the construction and repair of buildings.
(1980 Code, § 13-3) (Ord. passed 5-26-1903) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.04 GARBAGE, FILTH AND THE LIKE PROHIBITED ON STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND
ALLEYS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to place or cause to be placed or dumped
any and all garbage, filth or other nauseous or deleterious substances in or on
the streets, sidewalks and alleys of the town.
(1980 Code, § 13-4) (Ord. passed 5-26-1903) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.05 LOCAL ROAD AND STREET ACCOUNT.
   (A)   There is hereby created a special fund to be known as the Local Road
and Street Account into which the gasoline taxes allocated to the town by
reason of I.C. 8-14-2-1(b) shall be deposited. Such account shall be a
cumulative fund and all of the moneys deposited into such fund shall be
appropriated and used solely for local road and street improvements as
hereinafter defined, and none of such moneys shall revert to the General Fund
or be used for any purpose other than local road and street improvements.
   (B)   It is hereby declared to be the intent of the town that the moneys
deposited in the Local Road and Street Account shall be used exclusively for
engineering, land acquisition and construction on any road or street within the
jurisdiction of the town.
(1980 Code, § 13-5) (Ord. 3-1973, passed - -)
§ 90.06 ADA TRANSITION PLAN FOR PUBLIC RIGHTS-OF-WAY ADOPTED.
   The Americans with Disabilities Act’s, being 42 U.S.C. §§ 12101 et seq.
transition plan for public rights-of-way is hereby adopted by reference and
incorporated herein as if set out in full.
(Res. 8-2012, passed - -2012)
§ 90.07 STREET PARADE REQUEST PROCEDURE.
   The following procedures should be followed for any street parade request:
   (A)   Contact the Brookville Police Department Chief of Police at least 20
days prior to date of requested street parade for notification and approval;
   (B)   Present a detailed description of the proposed street parade request,
including organizer's contact information, purpose of street parade, and
proposed date/time;
   (C)   Present a simple diagram indicating the start/end of proposed street
parade request so Brookville Chief of Police may coordinate with Town
Departments and other local/county agencies; and
   (D)   Upon approval, organizer shall sign an indemnification clause agreeing
to defend and hold the Town of Brookville, its officers, agents, employees and
volunteers harmless from any claims, liabilities, losses or expenses arising
from said street parade.
(Res. 2020-4, passed 8-25-2020)
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any owner, occupant or other person violating the provisions of §
90.02 of this chapter shall, upon conviction, be punished as provided in §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
(1980 Code, § 13-2) (Ord. 4-1970, passed - -)
CHAPTER 91: HEALTH AND SANITATION; NUISANCES
Section
General Provisions
   
91.01   Chapter supplemental to other provisions relating to nuisances
   
91.02   Nuisances prohibited within town
   
91.03   Certain nuisances enumerated; enumeration not exclusive
   
91.04   Responsibility of property owners, occupants and others
Nuisance Abatement
   
91.15   Inspections, investigations and complaints
   
91.16   Right to enter private premises; duty of occupants
   
91.17   Notice to cease and desist when activity constitutes nuisance
   
91.18   Notice to abate condition constituting nuisance; appeal
   
91.19   Recourse of town when notice to abate nuisance ignored
   
91.20   Subchapter not to prohibit arrest for committing or maintaining

Weeds
   
91.40   Short title

91.43   Owners responsible for trimming, removal and the like
