CHAPTER 110: SOLICITORS
Section
   
110.01   Application for registration; requirements
   
110.02   Bond
   
110.03   Fee
   
110.04   Exemptions
§ 110.01 APPLICATION FOR REGISTRATION; REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to solicit for the sale of, offer
for sale or sell from house to house or by telephone within the corporate
limits of the town any goods, wares or merchandise whatsoever or subscriptions
to any kind of publication without first having registered with the Clerk-
Treasurer and having received a registration certificate showing the following:
      (1)   The name and home address of the applicant;
      (2)   The name and home address of the person such applicant represents
or in whose behalf he or she is soliciting orders or selling anything of value;
and
      (3)   A photograph of the applicant attached to such certificate and the
certification of the Clerk-Treasurer over his or her signature that such person
is registered.
   (B)   At the time of registration, the applicant shall furnish the Clerk-
Treasurer with two photographs of a size to be prescribed by the Clerk-
Treasurer. One of such photographs shall be attached to the certificate issued
to the registrant and the other shall be retained in the office of the Clerk-
Treasurer.
   (C)   At the time of registering, the applicant shall also submit to the
taking of a set of fingerprints by the Town Police Department and shall sign
his or her name to the registration record kept by the Clerk-Treasurer and to
the card on which his or her fingerprints are taken.
(1980 Code, § 12-1) (Ord. 3-1967, passed - -1967) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 110.02 BOND.
   Prior to the issuance of a certificate of registration, the applicant shall
file with the Clerk-Treasurer a bond in the penal sum of $1,000 with surety to
be approved by the Clerk-Treasurer conditioned upon his or her faithful and
honest discharge of the duties and obligations which he or she may undertake
between himself or herself and his or her customers and holding free from
financial loss anyone dealing with him or her.
(1980 Code, § 12-2) (Ord. 3-1967, passed - -1967)
§ 110.03 FEE.
   The Clerk-Treasurer shall collect from each person registered as a door-to-
door salesperson or solicitor, at the time of registration, a registration fee
in the sum of $25 for 30 days or $100 per year.
(1980 Code, § 12-3) (Ord. 3-1967, passed - -1967)
§ 110.04 EXEMPTIONS.
   There shall be exempted from the provisions of this chapter such local
civic, charitable, religious and non-profit organizations who from time to time
conduct solicitations and sell subscriptions and other items for the benefit of
such organizations, but not for the personal gain or benefit of any individual
connected therewith; provided that, each such organization prior to the
commencement of such solicitation or sales shall first make application with
the Clerk-Treasurer for an exemption certificate. Upon a showing being made to
the Clerk-Treasurer that the project and organization is qualified under the
exemption provisions of this chapter, such exemption certificate shall be
issued.
(1980 Code, § 12-4) (Ord. 3-1967, passed - -1967)
CHAPTER 111: SIDEWALK DINING
Section
   
111.01   Definitions
   
111.02   Application for registration; requirements
   
111.03   Restrictions
   
111.04   Renewal of registration
 
   
111.99   Penalty
§ 111.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purposes of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ABUTTING RETAIL BUSINESS PROPERTY. Any real property used for retail
business, which abuts (but is not located in) the public sidewalk area.
   CAFE AREA. The area used for dining activity and shall include the entire
sidewalk sales area and any part of the abutting retail business property used
directly for dining activity.
   DINING ACTIVITY. The retail sale of beverages or food or the provision of a
place for the consumption of beverages or food.
   EFFECTIVE WALKWAY WIDTH. The portion of the sidewalk in the public sidewalk
area that is reasonably available for use by the pedestrian stream moving
through the area.
   PUBLIC SIDEWALK AREA. A sidewalk area located in the public right-of-way or
in an area in which the public has an easement for sidewalk purposes, or both.
   SIDEWALK SALES AREA. The portion of the public sidewalk area which has been
registered with the town for dining activity.
(Ord. 2021-4, passed 3-23-2021)
§ 111.02 APPLICATION FOR REGISTRATION; REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   The Town Administrator is authorized to approve a registration of a
person to use a sidewalk sales area situated immediately next to the abutting
retail business property owned or leased by the person for the sole purpose of
engaging in dining activity. The sidewalk sales area which the person utilizes
for dining activity shall be located in the town. A person who desires to
register to use a sidewalk sales area for dining activity shall complete a
registration form provided by the Town Administrator, and file the form with
the Town Clerk.
   (B)   The Town Administrator shall approve the registration and issue a
certificate of registration to each applicant who qualifies, if the
requirements listed in this division are met.
      (1)   The sidewalk sales area shall meet these requirements:
         (a)   The sidewalk sales area must be located next to abutting retail
business property; and
         (b)   The dimensions of the sidewalk sales area to be used for dining
activity shall be approved by the Town Administrator, who shall determine the
effective walkway width that will safely and comfortably accommodate pedestrian
traffic at that location for a significant number of hours each week.
      (2)   The applicant shall be actively engaged in a retail business
involving the sale of beverages or food in the abutting retail business
property. The beverages or food sold in the cafe area will also be sold in the
abutting retail business property.
      (3)   The Town Administrator shall have determined on which days and
during what hours the sidewalk sales area may be used for dining activity.
      (4)   The applicant shall have provided a certificate of public liability
insurance to the Town Administrator insuring the person and naming the Town of
Brookville as an additional insured and be given a 30-day notice of
cancellation, non-renewal or significant change of coverage. The required
amounts of personal injury and property damage insurance requirements shall be
in the amount of $1,000,000 and shall be maintained by the applicant throughout
the term of the registration.
      (5)   The applicant shall have provided a document in which the applicant
agrees to indemnify and hold harmless the town for losses, damages, claims or
expenses arising out of the use of the sidewalk sales area for dining activity.
      (6)   A drawing or site plan which shows the appearance and location of
furniture, fixtures, and equipment (including, but not limited to, tables,
barriers, chairs, signs, awnings, trash receptacles and umbrellas) in the cafe
area shall have been approved by the Town Administrator for consistency with
the requirements and objectives of this chapter.
   (C)   All applications for licenses shall contain the following information:
      (1)   The applicant's name and business address;
      (2)   The name and address of all persons who have a financial or legal
interest in the business;
      (3)   The name in which, and address where, the business will be
conducted;
      (4)   Any other information specifically required by following sections
of this chapter, or deemed necessary by the Town Administrator; and
      (5)   All applications shall be signed personally by the applicant, and
if the applicant is not a
natural person, then by an authorized representative of the applicant.
(Ord. 2021-4, passed 3-23-2021)
§ 111.03 RESTRICTIONS.
   Use of the cafe area for dining activity under this chapter shall be subject
to the following conditions:
   (A)   The applicant shall be required to obey the commands of police
officers, firefighters and all other public authorities acting pursuant to law
with respect to activity carried out in the sidewalk sales area, including the
temporary removal of furniture and equipment and temporary cessation of dining
activity;
   (B)   When dining activity is not being conducted or when the abutting
retail business property is not open, the applicant shall remove from the
sidewalk sales area, or otherwise secure, all furniture, equipment and goods
which are susceptible to movement by the elements or by unauthorized persons;
   (C)   All furniture, equipment and goods must be susceptible to being
removed from the sidewalk sales area within a reasonable period of time at any
time with the manpower normally available to the applicant;
   (D)   Provision shall be made to assure the public area will not be
littered, including placement of adequate trash receptacles and periodic
picking up of litter in the sidewalk sales area;
   (E)   Pedestrians shall not be exposed to any undue safety or health hazard
nor shall a public nuisance be created;
   (F)   Sales of beverages or food may not be made to a person in or on any
motorized vehicle;
   (G)   Beverages or food sold in the sidewalk sales area shall be provided
only for consumption in the sidewalk sales area or in the abutting retail
business property;
   (H)   No alcohol may be served, sold or consumed on the sidewalk unless
authorized under the licensee's alcoholic beverage permit issued by the Indiana
Alcohol and Tobacco Commission. Proof of permission must be submitted with the
application;
   (I)   The Town Administrator, by written notice to the applicant, forbids
the use of the sidewalk sales area during the time and within the geographic
boundaries of a special event, or requires that the registrant meet the
additional requirements imposed on all vendors by the special event sponsor;
and
   (J)   A registration under this chapter shall be valid for a period of one
year from the date of issuance, unless a shorter period of time is indicated on
the certificate of registration.
(Ord. 2021-4, passed 3-23-2021)
§ 111.04 RENEWAL OF REGISTRATION.
   (A)   Prior to the time a registration under this chapter is renewed:
      (1)   The Town Administrator may review the pedestrian traffic flow and,
if appropriate, modify the sidewalk sales area or the days and hours the area
may be used;
      (2)   The Town Administrator may review the site plan to assure that any
changed conditions comply with the objectives of this chapter; and
      (3)   The Town Administrator may review the operation of the sidewalk
dining to determine if its continued operation is in the best interests of the
town.
   (B)   If, after the reviews provided in division (A) of this section, it is
determined that there are no changed conditions which would not allow
pedestrian traffic flow standards to be met, no changes in the detailed site
plan, and no change of circumstances such that the continued operation of the
sidewalk dining would not be in the best interests of the town, then
registrations shall be renewed automatically by the Town Administrator and
without application for renewal by the applicant, unless at the time of the
renewal the registration:
      (1)   Has been revoked or suspended; or
      (2)   Is the subject of administrative or judicial proceedings which have
the potential to result in the revocation or suspension of the registration, in
which case the registration may continue in effect until the conclusion of the
administrative or judicial proceedings.
   (C)   The Town Administrator shall be responsible for making periodic
inspections of dining activity carried out in sidewalk areas.
(Ord. 2021-4, passed 3-23-2021)
§ 111.99 PENALTY.
   Penalties for violation of this chapter include a fine of not less than $25
and, if the violation continues, a charge not less than $25 but no more than
$500 a day for each offense. A separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Ord. 2021-4, passed 3-23-2021)
CHAPTER 112: DESIGNATED OUTDOOR
REFRESHMENT AREAS
Section
   
112.01   Incorporation of state law
   
112.02   Total designated outdoor refreshment areas
   
112.03   Amendment or revocation of DORAs
   
112.04   Downtown DORA boundary
   
112.05   Compliance with zoning code
   
112.06   DORA signage and logo
   
112.07   Hours of operation
   
112.08   Designated permittee
   
112.09   Proposed designated permittees of downtown DORA
   
112.10   Trash receptacles
   
112.11   Special event application
   
112.12   Right of refusal
   
112.13   Permitted amounts
   
112.14   Authorized containers
   
112.15   Carryout businesses
   
112.16   Prohibition of outside beverages
   
112.17   Revocation of designated permittee and/or vendor status
 
   
112.99   Penalty
§ 112.01 INCORPORATION OF STATE LAW.
   All requirements and definitions found in I.C. 7.1-3-31 et seq., as the same
may be amended from time to time, shall apply throughout this chapter and are
incorporated herein by reference. If there exists a conflict between the
Indiana Code and this chapter, the provisions found in the Indiana Code shall
govern.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.02 TOTAL DESIGNATED OUTDOOR REFRESHMENT AREAS.
   The Town of Brookville (the "town") does hereby authorize the establishment
of a designated outdoor refreshment area ("DORA"), as described and detailed
herein, with the potential to establish a total of seven DORAs in and
throughout the town, as permitted by state law, at a point in the future as may
be determined by the Town Council.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.03 AMENDMENT OR REVOCATION OF DORAS.
   The town may, at any time, by subsequent ordinance of the Town Council,
amend or revoke a DORA, individually, or all DORAs in their entirety.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.04 DOWNTOWN DORA BOUNDARY.
   The downtown area depicted in Exhibit "A," attached to Ordinance 2024-10 is
incorporated herein by reference and is designated as the Downtown Brookville
designated outdoor refreshment area (the "downtown DORA"). The boundaries of
the downtown DORA by street address and direction are as follows: Beginning at
the intersection of Cliff Street and West 9th Street (Border #1) and moving
east to the intersection of 9th Street and Main Street (Border #2). Thence
moving south to the intersection of Main Street and 5th Street (Border #3) and
then east to the intersection of East 5th Street and Franklin Avenue (Border
#4). Moving then south to the intersection of Franklin Avenue and East 4th
Street (Border #5) and then west to the intersection of East 4th Street and an
unnamed alleyway (Border #6). South along said unnamed alleyway to the
intersection of the unnamed alleyway and East Third Street (Border #7). Then
moving west to the intersection of West 3rd Street and Progress Street (Border
#8) and then north to the intersection of Progress Street and West 7th Street
(Border #9) and then west to the intersection of West 7th Street and Cliff
Street (Border #10). Finally, from Border #10 north along Cliff Street to its
intersection with East 9th Street at Border #1.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.05 COMPLIANCE WITH ZONING CODE.
   The Town Council finds that the adoption of the downtown DORA and/or other
DORAs subsequently established are consistent with the Franklin County Zoning
Ordinance.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.06 DORA SIGNAGE AND LOGO.
   (A)   The Town Council hereby approves and adopts the signage requirements
of I.C. 7.1-3-31 et seq. designating the town's downtown DORA and said signage
shall be placed at the boundaries of the downtown DORA at common and
conspicuous exit locations as required by law. Said signage is attached to
Ordinance 2024-10, marked as Exhibit "B," and is incorporated herein by
reference.
   (B)   The Town Council shall adopt and establish a common brand or logo (the
"DORA logo") for the downtown DORA and other DORAs subsequently established.
Any and all private signage utilizing the DORA logo shall be subject to the
approval of the Town Council and shall comply with all applications and fees so
determined or required.
   (C)   All signage posted in furtherance of the downtown DORA and/or other
DORAs subsequently established shall comply with all signage requirements of
the town as enumerated by this code and/or the Franklin County Zoning
Ordinance. However, reasonable accommodations and deference shall be made and
afforded to enable and ensure signage which is proper and compliant with I.C.
7.1-3-31 et seq.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.07 HOURS OF OPERATION.
   Hours of operation for all DORAs shall be Sunday through Saturday from 10:30
a.m. until closure as required by I.C. 7.1-3-1-14, as the same may be amended
from time to time.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.08 DESIGNATED PERMITTEE.
   (A)   Businesses, persons, entities, or organizations which are located
within a DORA wishing to continually participate by selling or distributing
alcoholic beverages shall be required to complete and submit an application and
pay all required costs and/or fees, if any so required, to the town in order to
participate in a DORA as a “designated permittee.” Once approved by the town,
applications shall be sent to the Indiana Alcohol and Tobacco Commission for
said Commission's approval.
   (B)   Upon approval of the downtown DORA and each designated permittee by
the Indiana Alcohol and Tobacco Commission, the designated permittees shall
remove all stanchions, fencing, or other means of separation previously
required by law from all common areas, sidewalks, and rights-of- way of the
town as provided in I.C. 7.1-3-31-15.
   (C)   As required by I.C. 7.1-3-31-16, a sign must be posted at each exit of
a designated permittee and on the licensed premises of a vendor that informs
the public of the following:
      (1)   Any open alcoholic beverage containers purchasing within the DORA
must remain within the DORA;
      (2)   Possessing an open container of alcoholic beverages in a motor
vehicle may constitute a Class C infraction under I.C. 9-30-15; and
      (3)   The dates the DORA will be open if the DORA has date restrictions.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.09 PROPOSED DESIGNATED PERMITTEES OF DOWNTOWN DORA.
   (A)   The proposed and/or potential designated permittees of the downtown
DORA, pending each's interest, application, and approval shall include, but not
be necessarily limited to:
      (1)   Veterans of Foreign Wars Post 2014.
      (2)   Brookville Bowling Lanes.
      (3)   Fraternal Order of Eagles #1129.
      (4)   Third Place.
      (5)   Pioneer Restaurant and Lounge.
      (6)   Knights of Columbus #1010.
      (7)   Hilltop Restaurant and Sports Dining.
   (B)   The town requests the Indiana Alcohol and Tobacco Commission to
approve each as a designated permittee upon submission of their completed
application to the town. Any additional licensed premises located within the
downtown DORA who desire to be designated as a designated permittee shall
submit the town's DORA designated permittee application to the town for
approval and recommendation by the Town Council.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.10 TRASH RECEPTACLES.
   A designated permittee approved by the town and the Indiana Alcohol Tobacco
Commission shall continuously maintain a trash receptacle(s) outside of all
main entrances to their premises at their sole cost and expense.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.11 SPECIAL EVENT APPLICATION.
   Any business, person, entity, or organization, which is either not a
designated permittee; or a designated permittee wishing to expand operations
and service into the DORA beyond its licensed premises, that desires to sell
alcoholic beverages during an event, festival, or otherwise within a DORA (both
of the foregoing are referred to herein as a “vendor”), shall submit a special
event application and pay all required fees, if any so determined, to the town
for review and approval by the Town Council. Prior to submission of the special
events application, the vendor shall provide, as an attachment to the
application, copies of the vendor's temporary beer or wine permit or their
catering permit and/or supplement thereto; temporary vendor designation, both
of which may be obtained from the Indiana Alcohol and Tobacco Commission; and
any other required documentation or items so requested. Vendors shall be
permitted to establish a location of service and participation anywhere within
a DORA which they have elected to participate, subject to review and approval
by the Town Council as part of the special event application process.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.12 RIGHT OF REFUSAL.
   (A)   Any business, residence, landlord, building owner, retailer, real
property owner, lessee, and/or other establishment located in a DORA may, on a
non-discriminatory basis, refuse to allow patrons to enter their premises,
building, property, or business with an alcoholic beverage. Any business or
premises which elects to exercise a right of refusal or to otherwise deny
patrons' entry with alcoholic beverages is recommended to post signage
indicating such election, but such signage shall not be required.
   (B)   The town maintains full authority and control over sidewalks, streets,
parks, and/or other common areas of the town and no person or entity may
prohibit or limit open and authorized containers in accordance with I.C. 7.1-3-
31 et seq. with a DORA in those spaces except for the town Police Department.
   (C)   All designated permittees and/or vendors shall continue to have the
right to refuse service to any patron, on a non-discriminatory basis, in their
sole discretion in compliance with Indiana law.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.13 PERMITTED AMOUNTS.
   Subject to the provisions of §
112.14, below, all designated permittees and/or vendors shall permit patrons to
exit a designated permittee's and/or vendor's premises with not more than two
open containers of an alcoholic beverage at a time. The contents of an open
container may not exceed the following amounts:
   (A)   Beer or flavored malt beverage of not more than 16 ounces;
   (B)   Wine, cider, or hard seltzer of not more than 12 ounces; and
   (C)   A mixed drink of not more than ten ounces which contains not more than
two ounces of liquor.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.14 AUTHORIZED CONTAINERS.
   (A)   Designated permittees and/or vendors shall only permit patrons to exit
their premises into a DORA if the alcoholic beverages, as described in §
112.13, above, have been placed into a clear, non-breakable plastic cup labeled
with a label approved and authorized by the town (“DORA container”). Beverages
consumed within a designated permittee's and/or vendor's premises are not
required to be in a DORA container. Glass containers are explicitly prohibited
from exiting the designated permittee's and/or vendor's premises into the DORA.
   (B)   The town may delegate and designate a department, agent, entity, and/
or individual with the right to serve as the exclusive designer, manufacturer,
distributor, and/or retailer and service provider of DORA containers for use in
DORAs and does so delegate said authority to Main Street Brookville, Inc., an
Indiana Non-Profit Corporation with 501(c)(3) status (Main Street).
   (C)   The town shall adopt and approve a DORA container, the design of which
shall include the DORA logo and/or any other information approved or so
required by the Town Council.
   (D)   Designated permittees and/or vendors shall purchase the requisite DORA
containers from Main Street. Main Street shall utilize proceeds obtained from
the sale of the authorized DORA containers to assist the town with the payment
of costs and expenses associated with signage, public safety, sanitation costs,
events, improvements, and operations of the downtown DORA and any subsequently
created DORAs.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.15 CARRYOUT BUSINESSES.
   The carryout privileges that a designated permittee may have under the scope
of the designated permittee's other permits or licenses shall not be impacted
or otherwise affected by being located within a DORA. Carryout of sealed
original containers shall be permitted, but said containers shall not be opened
or consumed within a DORA.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.16 PROHIBITION OF OUTSIDE BEVERAGES.
   Persons may not consume beverages within a DORA which were not purchased
from a designated permittee and/or vendor and/or which were brought from
outside a DORA into the subject DORA. The refilling of a DORA container with
alcoholic or other beverages is strictly prohibited.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.17 REVOCATION OF DESIGNATED PERMITTEE AND/OR VENDOR STATUS.
   Failure of a designated permittee and/or vendor to comply with this chapter
shall result in an immediate revocation of a designated permittee's and/or
vendor's right and ability to participate in a DORA. In such event, the town or
its designated department or agent shall deliver or serve notice of such
revocation upon the designated permittee and/or vendor. Said revocation shall
contain the following: that the revocation shall be immediately effective; that
the designated permittee and/or vendor shall immediately cease their
participation in the DORA; and the basis for the revocation. Said revocation
may be appealed to the Town Council by the designated permittee and/or vendor
by providing the Clerk-Treasurer of the town with a request to appear before
the Town Council at its next regularly scheduled meeting within ten calendar
days of the notice of revocation being delivered or served on the designated
permittee and/or vendor. If a revocation is reversed by the Town Council, the
designated permittee and/or vendor shall be subject to a fine as outlined in §
112.99, below. The decision of the Town Council regarding such appeal of a
revocation shall be final.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)
§ 112.99 PENALTY.
   In addition to the revocation outlined in §
112.16, above, any violation of any section of this chapter by any designated
permittee, vendor, person, business, and the like shall be a Class C infraction
punishable by a fine of up to $500.
(Ord. 2024-10, passed 6-17-2024)

*** GENERAL PROVISIONS ***
§ 130.01 AIR GUNS; DISCHARGE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to shoot or discharge, or to permit any
other person to shoot or discharge, any air gun within the town.
(1980 Code, § 11-1) (Ord. passed 1-11-1926) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 EXPLOSIVES; USE WITHIN TOWN.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to use dynamite or other explosives, in
any form whatever, within the town.
(1980 Code, § 11-3) (Ord. passed 7-22-1895) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.03 FIREARMS; DISCHARGE.
   No person shall fire off, within the town, any firearm.
(1980 Code, § 11-4) (Ord. passed 7-22-1895) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.04 CURFEW.
   (A)   It is a curfew violation for a child 15, 16 or 17 years of age to be
in a public place:
      (1)   Between 1:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m. on Saturday or Sunday;
      (2)   After 11:00 p.m. on Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday or Thursday;
or
      (3)   Before 5:00 a.m. on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday or Friday.
   (B)   It is a curfew violation for a child less than 15 years of age to be
in a public place after 11:00 p.m. or before 5:00 a.m. on any day.
   (C)   It is a defense to a violation of divisions (A) or (B) above that, at
the time the child engaged in the prohibited conduct, the child was
emancipated:
      (1)   Under I.C. 31-37-19-27 or I.C. 31-6-4-15.7 (before its repeal);
      (2)   By virtue of having married; or
      (3)   In accordance with the laws of another state or jurisdiction.
   (D)   It is a defense to a violation of divisions (A) or (B) above that the
child engaged in the prohibited conduct while:
      (1)   Accompanied by the child’s parent, guardian or custodian;
      (2)   Accompanied by an adult specified by the child’s parent, guardian
or custodian;
      (3)   Participating in, going to or returning from:
         (a)   Lawful employment;
         (b)   A school sanctioned activity;
         (c)   A religious event;
         (d)   An emergency involving the protection of a person or property
from an imminent threat of serious bodily injury or substantial damage;
         (e)   An activity involving the exercise of the child’s rights
protected under the First Amendment to the United States Constitution or Art.
1, § 31, of the Constitution of the state, or both, such as freedom of speech
and the right of assembly; or
         (f)   An activity conducted by a non-profit or governmental entity
that provides recreation, education, training or other care under the
supervision of one or more adults.
      (4)   Engaged in interstate or international travel from a location
outside the state to another location outside the state.
(Ord. 1-2001, passed 6-26-2001) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.05 NOISE.
   (A)   Unnecessary noise generally. Subject to the provisions of this
section, the creation of any unreasonably loud, disturbing and unnecessary
noise is prohibited. Noise of such character, intensity and duration as to be
detrimental to the life or health of any individual is prohibited.
(1980 Code, § 9-1)
   (B)   Noises expressly prohibited. The following acts, among others, are
declared to be loud, disturbing and unnecessary noises in violation of this
section, but such enumeration shall not be deemed to be exclusive:
      (1)   The sounding of any horn or signal device on any automobile,
motorcycle, bus or other vehicle while not in motion, except as a danger signal
if another vehicle is approaching apparently out of control, or if in motion,
only as a danger signal after or as brakes are being applied and deceleration
of the vehicle is intended; the creation by means of any such signal device of
any unreasonably loud or harsh sound; and the sounding of such device for an
unnecessary and unreasonable period of time;
      (2)   The use of any gong or siren upon any vehicle other than a police,
fire or other emergency vehicle;
      (3)   The playing of any radio, phonograph or other musical instrument in
such manner or with such volume, particularly during the hours between 11: 00
p.m. and 7: 00 a.m., as to annoy or disturb the quiet, comfort or repose of any
person in any dwelling, hotel or other type of residence;
      (4)   The keeping of any animal or bird which, by causing frequent or
long continued noise, shall disturb the comfort and repose of any person in the
vicinity;
      (5)   The use of any automobile, motorcycle or other vehicle so out of
repair, so loaded or in such manner as to create loud or unnecessary grating,
grinding, rattling or other noise;
      (6)   The blowing of any steam whistle attached to any stationary boiler,
except to give notice of the time to begin or stop work or as a warning of
danger;
      (7)   The discharge into the open air of the exhaust of any steam engine,
stationary internal combustion engine, motor vehicle or motorboat engine,
except through a muffler or other device which will effectively prevent loud or
explosive noises therefrom;
      (8)   The use of any mechanical device operated by compressed air, unless
the noise created thereby is effectively muffled and reduced;
      (9)   The erection, including excavation, demolition, alteration or
repair of any building in a residential or business district, other than
between the hours of 7:00 a.m. and 6:00 p.m. on weekdays, except in the case of
urgent necessity in the interest of public safety, and then only with a permit
from the Council, which permit may be renewed for a period of three days or
less while the emergency continues;
      (10)   The creation of any excessive noise on any street adjacent to any
school, institution of learning or court while the same is in session, or
within 150 feet of any hospital, which unreasonably interferes with the working
of such institution; provided that, conspicuous signs are displayed in such
street indicating that the same is a school, court or hospital street;
      (11)   The creation of any excessive noise on Sundays on any street
adjacent to any church; provided that, conspicuous signs are displayed in such
streets adjacent to churches indicating that the same are church streets;
      (12)   The creation of loud and excessive noise in connection with
loading or unloading any vehicle or the opening and destruction of bales,
boxes, crates and containers;
      (13)   The sounding of any bell or gong attached to any building or
premises, which disturbs the quiet or repose of any person in the vicinity
thereof;
      (14)   The shouting and crying of peddlers, barkers, hawkers and vendors,
which disturbs the quiet and peace of the neighborhood;
      (15)   The use of any drum, loudspeaker or other instrument or device for
the purpose of attracting attention by creation of noise to any performance,
show or sale or display of merchandise;
      (16)   The use of any mechanical loudspeakers or amplifiers on trucks or
other moving vehicles for advertising or other purposes, except where a
specific license is received from the Police Department; and
      (17)   The conducting, operating or maintaining of any garage or filling
station in any residential district so as to cause loud or offensive noises to
be emitted therefrom between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m.
(1980 Code, § 9-2) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.06 TOWN PARKS TOBACCO-FREE POLICY.
   (A)   Tobacco use is hereby eliminated in Brookville Town Parks. No person
shall use any form of tobacco including cigarettes, cigars, vaping devices,
electronic cigarettes, and other smokeless tobacco at or on any Brookville Town
Park including the restrooms, spectator and concession areas. These facilities
include the entire park area, but are not limited to, playgrounds, athletic
fields, parks, shelters and walking/hiking trails.
   (B)   Enforcement.
      (1)   Appropriate signs shall be posted in the above specified areas.
      (2)   The community will be notified about this policy.
      (3)   Any person found violating this policy will be asked to discontinue
tobacco use or remove themselves from the premises of the Brookville Town
Parks.
(Res. 7-2016, passed 11-8-2016)
*** LITTERING ***
§ 130.20 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   LITTER. Any human-made or human-used waste, which, if deposited within the
town otherwise than in a litter receptacle, tends to create a danger to public
health, safety and welfare or to impair the environment of the people of the
town. LITTER shall include, but is not limited to, garbage, trash, refuse,
debris, grass clippings or other lawn or garden waste, paper products, glass,
metal, plastic or paper containers, motor vehicle parts, furniture, carcasses
of dead animals or any other waste material of any unsightly, unsanitary,
nauseous or offensive nature.
   PRIVATE PREMISES. All property, including, but not limited to, vacant land
or any land upon which is located one or more buildings or other structure
designed or used for residential, commercial, business, industrial,
institutional or religious purposes, together with any yard, grounds, walk,
driveway, fence, porch or other structure or improvement appurtenant to said
land, except any public place.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any and all streets, curbs and gutters, sidewalks, alleys or
other public ways, and any and all public parks, lakes, spaces, grounds or
buildings within the corporate limits of said town or owned by said town.
(Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994)
§ 130.21 MAINTENANCE OF PRIVATE PROPERTY; FREE OF LITTER.
   The owner or person in control of any private premises shall, at all times,
maintain such private premises free of litter that is capable of being carried
or deposited by the elements upon any public place or other private premises.
(Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.22 LITTERING FROM VEHICLES.
   (A)   No person, while the operator of or a passenger in a vehicle, shall
deposit litter upon any public place or private premises.
   (B)   No person shall drive or move any loaded or partially loaded truck or
other vehicle within the town unless the vehicle is so constructed or loaded as
to prevent any part of its load, contents or litter from being blown or
deposited upon any public place or private premises.
(Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.23 GRASS CLIPPINGS.
   No person shall discharge or permit the discharge of grass clippings upon
any public street, alley or any surface water gutter or drain in the town.
(Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.24 TAMPERING WITH RECEPTACLES.
   No person shall upset or tamper with public or private receptacles designed
or used for the deposit of litter.
(Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994) Penalty, see §
130.99
*** LOITERING ***
§ 130.35 LOITERING PROHIBITED.
   No person shall loiter or prowl in a place, at a time or in a manner not
usual for law abiding citizens, under circumstances that warrant a justifiable
and reasonable alarm or immediate concern for the safety of persons or property
in the vicinity, in any public way, street, highway, place or alley and refuse
to obey the lawful command of a police officer to move on or provide to said
police officer a lawful reason for remaining on said public way, street,
highway, place or alley if the alleged loitering by said person would create or
cause to be created any of the following:
   (A)   Danger of a breach of the peace;
   (B)   The unreasonable danger of a disturbance to the comfort and repose of
any person acting lawfully on or in a public way, street, highway, place or
alley reserved for pedestrians;
   (C)   The obstruction or attempted obstruction of the free normal flow of
vehicular traffic or the normal passage of pedestrian traffic upon any public
way, street, highway, place or alley; and/or
   (D)   The obstruction, molestation or interference or attempt to obstruct,
molest or interfere with any person lawfully on or in a public way, street,
highway, place or alley, in a manner that would cause a reasonable person or
pedestrian of a public way, street, highway place or alley to fear for his or
her safety.
(Ord. 3-1990, passed 8-28-1990) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.36 UNLAWFUL ASSEMBLY PROHIBITED.
   No person who is a member of a group of three or more persons who are
loitering or prowling in a place, at a time or in a manner not usual for law
abiding citizens, under circumstances that warrant a justifiable and reasonable
alarm or immediate concern for the safety of persons or property in the
vicinity, in a public way, street, highway, place or alley, shall refuse the
lawful command of a police officer to move on or provide to said police officer
a lawful reason for remaining in a public way, street, highway, place or alley,
whether said group is stationary or in transit, if the alleged loitering would
create or cause to be created any of the following:
   (A)   Danger of a breach of the peace;
   (B)   The unreasonable danger of a disturbance to the comfort and repose of
any person acting lawfully on or in a public way, street, highway, place or
alley reserved for pedestrians;
   (C)   The obstruction or attempted obstruction of the free normal flow of
vehicular traffic or the normal passage of pedestrian traffic upon any public
way, street, highway, place or alley; and/or
   (D)   The obstruction, molestation or interference or attempt to obstruct,
molest or interfere with any person lawfully on or in a public way, street,
highway, place or alley, in a manner that would cause a reasonable person or
pedestrian of a public way, street, highway place or alley to fear for his or
her safety.
(Ord. 3-1990, passed 8-28-1990) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.37 EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   No person shall be convicted under this subchapter if the police
officer failed to comply with the procedure outlined herein.
   (B)   No person shall be convicted under this subchapter if it appears at
trial that the explanation given by the person is true and, if believed by the
police would:
      (1)   Have dispelled the fear for human safety;
      (2)   Have dispelled the concern for safety of property;
      (3)   Have dispelled the fear of a breach of the peace; and
      (4)   Have provided a justifiable reason for obstructing vehicular or
pedestrian traffic, subject to the discretion of the court.
   (C)   The provisions of this subchapter shall not apply to solicitation for
any lawful business or any solicitor licensed under the provisions of Ord. 6-
1977.
(Ord. 3-1990, passed 8-28-1990)
§ 130.38 PROBABLE CAUSE.
   If a person takes flight upon appearance of a police officer who identifies
himself or herself as such, or refuses to identify himself or herself, or
attempts to conceal himself or herself, said police officer has probable cause
to believe a violation of this section has occurred and is hereby duly
authorized to make an arrest.
(Ord. 3-1990, passed 8-28-1990)
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any person, firm or corporation violating any of §§
130.20 through
130.24 of this chapter may be served by an authorized enforcement person with a
notice of violation. The person upon whom a notice of violation is served may
admit liability to the violation and pay a civil penalty of $25 directly to the
town.
      (2)   If, in the opinion of the authorized enforcement person, the
violation is so substantial as to warrant a more severe penalty, the authorized
enforcement person may issue a notice of violation and citation to appear in
the County Circuit Court for determination of the alleged violation of §§
130.20 through
130.24 of this chapter.
      (3)   Except as otherwise provided herein, any person found in violation
of the provisions of §§
130.20 through
130.24 of this chapter shall be fined an amount not to exceed $2,500 for each
violation. Each day in violation shall be considered a separate violation. The
town may also seek an injunction to prevent further violation of §§
130.20 through
130.24 of this chapter.
   (C)   Any person, firm or corporation convicted of violating any provision
of §§
130.35 through
130.38 of this chapter shall be fined not less than $25, nor more than $500,
for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on each day
during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Ord. 3-1990, passed 8-28-1990; Ord. 8-1994, passed 11-22-1994)
